**✅ 2026-09-16: KAYLIN'S FIVE LOGIN ROWS — CLEANED UP AND VERIFIED (`fe1c615`).** Found while debugging the PTO approve button, so it was NOT the bug Andrew reported, but it was a real one.

```
40bc9046  kaylin@builtthings.com   admin   Built            signed in 09-15 · 48 tasks · ON ROSTER  ← KEEP
9b4992d2  kaylin@placeholder.com   member  Watson cab shop  no auth_user_id, ever
23ddf6be  kaylin@builtthings.com   owner   Built (test)     auth account already deleted
de37bc22  kaylinprice23@gmail.com  owner   KP               auth account already deleted
251edd4e  kaylinprice23@gmail.com  member  Built            LIVE · zero refs · linked to NO roster row
```
- ⚠️ **`kaylin@builtthings.com` exists TWICE across two different auth accounts.** The live one is the roster's.
- **The last row explains the confusion:** signing in with the gmail address lands her in Built as a member linked to no roster row — so `/me` disowns her and anything keyed to the roster looks empty. **Andrew's call: delete it.**
- ✅ **DONE.** Andrew ran `scripts/fix-duplicate-logins.mjs --apply`; all four deleted. Re-running `inspect-duplicate-logins` shows **exactly one row**, with its 48 tasks and roster link intact. The scripts stay — `inspect-duplicate-logins.mjs` takes a name argument (`npx tsx scripts/inspect-duplicate-logins.mjs smith`) and is the tool for the next report of this.
- ⛔ **THE LIST IS THE INTENT; THE GUARD IS THE AUTHORITY.** A `users.id` is stamped on tasks, time entries, comments and accepted change orders, and the list came from a snapshot — so the script **re-counts references and roster links per row at run time and refuses that row** if either is non-empty. The keeper is named in a `KEEP` const the loop refuses outright. The users row deletes BEFORE the auth account, so a failure leaves a consistent pair rather than an auth login with no profile. **Auth accounts cannot be un-deleted.**

All three now report, in the banner, next to the button. Deny and delete had the same zero-row trap. ⚠️ **If it still fails for Kaylin it will now SAY WHY** — that message is the next clue.

**NEW 2026-09-15 (two Cowork design passes with Andrew): CHANGE ORDERS V2 — scoped, NOT built. THE BIG ONE; build after the time/schedule batch.** The CO stops being a guess-the-number modal and becomes a **draft revision of the project, priced by the real composer**: one open CO doc per project accumulating changes until accepted → locked; drafts live visibly ON the project as highlighted subs (install-sub flag pattern); additions price at current rates, credits at original contract value; **QB hears nothing until acceptance, then one separate CO invoice per doc** (Andrew's call). Full spec = "Change Orders v2" in Now. Supersedes the v1 authoring UI when it ships; v1 history stays readable.

### Change Orders v2 — spec (scoped 2026-09-15)

**Why:** the v1 modal (spec-change delta or custom $+hours) isn't linked to the rate book — Andrew: "I'm just guessing at the costs." His Pajot case: ONE CO on the island = remove the rounded end panel + add a finish panel + add a solid-wood waterfall top — three edits, one CO, and today that's multiple guessed COs.

**Core model — one open doc, drafts on the project:**
1. **One OPEN CO doc per project** (CO #N). It accumulates every pending change, its PDF regenerates freely, and everything stays editable **until ACCEPTED** (in-app mark or the portal's existing CO signature fields from 092) → doc locks, drafts APPLY, the next change opens doc #N+1. Repeat cycles are first-class.
2. **Drafts are visible, highlighted, and inert to production:**
   - **New sub via CO** — a real subproject flagged as CO-draft (the install-sub flag pattern: color + behavior). Excluded from bid_total, schedule, capacity, pre-production gating and shop lists until accepted; removable from the doc at any time. Andrew's ask that a whole NEW scope (e.g. a new vanity) arrives as a new sub — confirmed.
   - **Inline edit to an existing sub** — the CO **reopens the sub in the composer**; edits save as a DRAFT REVISION attached to the doc (contract lines stay the working truth; the sub shows a "CO pending" highlight until resolved). Delete lines, add lines, change slots — all real rate-book pricing.
   - **Delete a whole sub** — marked-for-removal highlight; on acceptance it ARCHIVES (it may hold tracked hours), never hard-deletes.
3. **Pricing basis (the money rule):** additions/new lines price at the **current** rate book with the project's margin; removals credit at **original contract value**; a modification = credit old + add new. **Frozen/imported bases NEVER reprice** (the handoff-repricing lesson). Delta per doc item is stored, not derived at render.
4. **QB:** nothing before acceptance. On acceptance the doc pushes **one separate QB invoice** ("{project} — CO #N", linked `qbo_invoice_id`); the watcher applies payments to it; **one labelled draw row appends for the doc total** (generalizing `596b201`). Internal-mode orgs mirror with a MillSuite invoice as usual.
5. **Acceptance effects, all at once:** drafts apply (lines update, draft subs become normal subs, removals archive) · est/dept hours update so schedule + capacity tell the truth · new materials/specs can spawn pre-production approval slots · PDF snapshots immutable · `project_events` row.
6. **The doc PDF:** per modified sub — a description of the change + the delta (credits as negative lines); additions as normal priced lines; new subs as their own sections. Standard template styling first; presentation variant later if Andrew asks.

**Data sketch (Code refines; next migration numbers from 107):** `co_docs` (project_id, number, status open|accepted|void, accepted_at/by, portal-sign link, qbo_invoice_id, pdf snapshot) · `co_doc_items` (doc_id, subproject_id NULL, kind add_sub|edit_sub|remove_sub, draft payload/diff jsonb, description, delta_amount, credit_basis) · ~~a CO-draft flag on subprojects for new-sub drafts~~ ⛔ **NOT BUILT — see the departure note below; drafts are jsonb, materialised on acceptance.** Draft revisions must key on the DOC id so an open doc #2 can't tangle with doc #1's applied history.

**Cutover:** v1 authoring retires when v2 ships; v1's accepted COs stay readable everywhere they show today; check for OPEN v1 COs at cutover and migrate or finish them by hand with Andrew. The scoped-and-ready "surface draw drift" item stays valid and separate.

**Build order inside the feature:** (1) doc + new-sub drafts + delete-sub + acceptance + PDF → (2) inline composer draft revisions (the hard part — the diff) → (3) QB push + draw append → (4) portal signature hookup. Each step shippable behind the previous.

#### ✅ STEP 1 COMPLETE — FOUNDATION (`d3a03e1`) + DRAFTS UI (`32f0477`) + PDF (`fca0805`) · ✅ **MIGRATION `107` IS ON PROD**

**⚠️ BOTH OPEN QUESTIONS ARE ANSWERED (2026-09-15).**
1. **Architecture — BLESSED, with a condition Andrew called non-negotiable.** Storing drafts as jsonb is fine, **but the on-screen behaviour is not negotiable: a pending CO renders ON THE PROJECT PAGE as highlighted sub cards** — a CO-added sub as a draft-coloured card, an edited sub with a "CO pending" highlight, a sub marked for removal visibly flagged — **all inert to `bid_total`, schedule, capacity and pre-production until the doc is accepted. If the payload model can't render that, switch to flagged subprojects.** It can: the project page reads `co_doc_items` explicitly and renders draft cards beside the real ones — one deliberate opt-in, which is the whole point of the payload shape. **Proving it is the next commit.**
2. **Imported pricing — FIXED FIRST, per Andrew, before any more CO work.** See the block immediately below.

#### ✅ 2026-09-15: THE IMPORT PRICE FREEZE NOW BELONGS TO THE SUBPROJECT (`b28bf57`) · ✅ **MIGRATION `108` IS ON PROD** (re-run of `inspect-freeze-repricing` against the real `price_frozen` values confirms nothing repriced)

**The bug:** `recomputeProjectBidTotal`, the project page, the subproject editor and the handoff page all read `projects.imported_at` and froze the **whole project** — shop rate 0, consumables 0, margins 0. Right for Built's migrated lines (they carry their quoted price verbatim as a material lump). **Wrong for anything added after the import:** a subproject created today on an imported job, with real composer lines and real hours, priced at **material cost with zero labor and zero margin.**

**Measured, not asserted** — `npx tsx scripts/inspect-freeze-repricing.mjs` re-prices each imported job's biggest hour-carrying sub as if a CO had just added it:
- **Hunt - Pajot Millwork → "TV Wall" (142.3h): $18,918 → $46,997** (+148%)
- Schiller - Brabson Dining Table → "Dining Table" (110h): $14,544 → $36,198
- Leonard - JW Marriott Lobby Bar → "Bar Top & Kneewall" (148h): $44,724 → $91,275
- Murtagh Bar → "Bar Island" (33h): $5,560 → $13,961 · across the imported book the gap runs **79–197%**.

**⛔ TWO MECHANISMS WERE STACKED, and fixing one alone still gives the work away.** (1) rate 0 + consumables 0 — now per-subproject via `isSubFrozen` + `subprojects.price_frozen` (108). (2) **the importer PINS every project margin to 0**, so `resolveBucketMargins` returns zeros for the whole job — `resolveMarginsForNewScope` ignores those pins for the live half. ⚠️ **Cost of (2): a margin pinned BY HAND on an imported job won't apply to new scope there** — indistinguishable from the importer's zeros. Safe side of the trade; the alternative is billing a CO at cost.

**⚠️ ONE PROJECT CAN NOW HOLD BOTH KINDS**, so margin can't be applied once over a single bucket sum — the frozen half would be marked up twice. Two accumulators through `priceMixedBuckets` (exact: `priceFromMargin` is linear), applied in **all four** pricing surfaces **including the QuickBooks line seeding**, because those numbers go on a client invoice.

**⛔ IT REPRICES NOTHING TODAY.** `inspect-freeze-repricing.mjs` half 1 reports every project computing an identical total before and after. The backfill freezes everything from Built (via `migration_id_map` — the importer's own record, not a heuristic) plus anything that existed at import; the only rows left live are **Batia's two post-import subprojects, which have no estimate lines.** It changes what happens to scope added from now on.

**⚠️ THE FALLBACK DIRECTION IS LOAD-BEARING.** An absent `price_frozen` — a caller that didn't select it — resolves to the OLD project-level answer (frozen). A forgotten select behaves as today instead of quietly re-pricing a signed contract. `project-totals` also **retries the select without the column**, because PostgREST fails the WHOLE select on one unknown column and the `subs.length === 0` guard would otherwise make a pre-108 database look like a page that simply didn't update.

**`scripts/verify-import-freeze.mjs`** — 29 checks, including the fallback direction and the "one sum through one margin" failure stated as a number ($24,000 bills Built twice, $12,000 gives the CO away, $14,000 is right).

**Built so far — libs + rules ONLY. No UI; nothing is reachable in the app yet.** `db/migrations/107_co_docs.sql` · `lib/co-doc-math.ts` (pure rules) · `lib/co-docs.ts` (IO: load / create / amend / accept / void) · `lib/composer-row.ts` (the shared line payload) · `scripts/verify-co-docs.mjs` (44 checks). tsc clean; the RLS audit exits 0 with both new tables registered (its `CREATE TABLE` scan caught them unlisted, as designed).

**⛔ ONE DELIBERATE DEPARTURE FROM THE SPEC ABOVE — VETO IT NOW WHILE IT'S CHEAP.** The spec says a new-scope draft is *"a real subproject flagged as CO-draft"*. **It isn't. A draft is a jsonb payload on `co_doc_items.draft`, materialised into a real subproject only on acceptance.** Reason: **`from('subprojects')` appears 60 times across 20+ files** — project page, schedule, capacity, handoff, pre-production, subproject editor, /time, /time/mobile, **the client portal**, the QB push, project-totals, closed-jobs, schedule-seed, the outcome API, the CO PDF route. A flag makes every one of those an **opt-out** that has to remember to exclude drafts forever; the one that forgets leaks scope the client never agreed to into the contract total, the production schedule, the capacity plan, or an invoice. **That is this codebase's recurring failure shape** — the hand-maintained RLS table list, `RESERVED_SLUGS`, the `!inner` join that hid $426k of Leonard work. The user-visible model is unchanged (drafts still render as highlighted sub cards), only the storage differs, and the spec's own data sketch already says *"draft payload/diff jsonb"*. Full argument is in 107's header. ⚠️ Cost: a draft sub can't be opened in the normal subproject editor by id until accepted — step 2 needs a payload-shaped diff anyway, so this aligns with that work.

**✅ SUPERSEDED — FIXED 2026-09-15 by `b28bf57` + migration `108`; see the block above. Kept because the reasoning still explains the CO pricing rules.** ⛔ **A FINDING THAT NEEDED ANDREW'S CALL, AND IT WAS NOT A CO BUG — IT WAS LIVE.** `recomputeProjectBidTotal` treats `imported_at` as a **project-level** price freeze (shop rate 0, consumables 0, every margin 0) because Built's migrated lines carry their quoted price verbatim as a material lump. Right for those lines. **But it applies to any subproject added AFTER the import too** — so new scope on an imported job (Schiller, Murtagh, Batia… most of the live book) prices at **material cost with zero labor and zero margin**. A CO that materialises a sub hits this head-on: the client signs $5,000 and the contract moves by the material figure alone.
- **Not decided unilaterally.** The obvious fix — freeze the imported *lines*, not the *project* — would reprice sold jobs. That's Andrew's call.
- **What's built instead:** additions price at **today's** rates (a CO is work quoted now); credits use the **project's own** rule, so an imported sub credits the frozen Built number the client actually paid (`credit_basis` records which). `acceptDoc` then measures `agreed − moved` and returns it as **`drift`** instead of absorbing it silently — same idea as the already-scoped "Surface `drift`" item.
- **⚠️ ANDREW, RUN THIS (read-only; my sandbox blocks service-role scripts):** `cd ~/code/millsuite-mvp-profit && node scripts/inspect-imported-pricing.mjs` — it lists imported projects that already have post-import subprojects and how many hours are priced at $0. **A non-empty list means money is being lost today, with no change order involved.**

**Fixed on the way (not CO-specific):** the composer's `estimate_lines` payload was built inline in two places in `lib/composer-persist.ts` — the one piece of logic deciding what the rollup later *sees*, duplicated and untestable. Now one function (`composerLineRow`) shared by save, update **and** CO materialisation, which is what makes a draft's quoted price and its post-acceptance price the same computation over the same object. ⚠️ **`formatThickness`/`quartersToInches` moved to `lib/solid-wood-units.ts`**: `lib/solid-wood` imports `lib/supabase` at module scope, so the composer built a database client just to format "8/4" and its pricing couldn't load in a verify script. **Re-exporting was NOT enough — the re-export keeps the chain, so `lib/composer.ts` had to import the pure file by name.** The next purity split will hit the same trap.

**✅ 2026-09-15: THE HIGHLIGHTED-DRAFTS UI IS BUILT (`32f0477`) — Andrew's condition is met, and the inert half is now ENFORCED rather than promised.**
- **Visible:** `CoDraftPanel` (the open doc: CO-0N, per-item cards, net delta, Accept/Void) renders under the subproject list. **New scope = purple dashed card. A sub flagged for removal turns the REAL subproject card red and struck through, with a "Remove in CO / Undo" toggle beside it. An `edit_sub` gets the amber "CO pending" card** (step 2 authors those; the rendering is already there).
- **Inert:** a draft is not a `subprojects` row, so the ~60 readers of that table cannot see it and none of them had to remember anything.
- ⛔ **`verify-co-docs` now SCANS THE TREE and fails if `co_docs`/`co_doc_items` is queried anywhere but `lib/co-docs.ts`.** The inertness holds exactly as long as drafts have ONE reader — a second query anywhere puts "remember to filter" back, which is the failure the payload model exists to avoid. ⚠️ **Confirmed the guard actually fails on a planted violation**; a check that can't fail is the bug this codebase has shipped twice already (the RLS table list, `RESERVED_SLUGS`).
- **Composer draft mode:** `subprojectId` accepts `null` and `draftMode.onComposed` hands back exactly what `saveComposerLine` would have written, so new scope is priced by the real rate book before any row exists. Draft edit hydrates from the payload, not from `estimate_lines`. The modal's price comes from `priceAddition` — the same function that stores it — so the quote can't disagree with the change order.
- ⛔ **Acceptance is NOT a transaction** (PostgREST offers none), so a run that materialises two of three subprojects and then fails leaves the doc open and re-acceptable. It now **skips items already carrying a `subproject_id`**, so a retry doesn't build the first two again and charge once for scope that exists twice.
- **`drift` is surfaced on acceptance** (agreed vs what the contract actually moved by) instead of being swallowed.

**✅ 2026-09-15: THE DOC PDF IS BUILT (`fca0805`) — STEP 1 IS COMPLETE.** `CoDocPdf` + `/api/co-docs/[id]/pdf` + a PDF button on the panel.
- ⛔ **A DOC IS NOT A CO.** The v1 PDF renders ONE change (before chip → after chip, one price). A v2 doc is the whole conversation — **Pajot is one change order with three edits to the same island** — so this renders a LIST with one net total and one signature, **credits as negative lines**, and new scope listing the composed lines behind its number.
- ⛔ **AN ACCEPTED DOC'S PDF IS IMMUTABLE, and the v1 route cannot do that** — it re-renders every request and upserts the same path. Fine for a v1 CO whose fields are frozen; **not** for a v2 doc, whose deltas were priced against a moving rate book and whose drafts are already real subprojects. Re-rendering would replace the signed document at the same URL. So: an accepted doc returns its stored `pdf_url` untouched, only an OPEN doc re-renders, and `pdf_url` is stamped **only once accepted** (stamping earlier freezes the first preview as the record). **Acceptance takes the snapshot itself**, best-effort, because the route only stamps when asked.
- ⛔ **ACCEPTED DOCS WERE UNREACHABLE** — a doc leaves the open panel the moment it's accepted, taking the signed document and the record of what was agreed with it. There's now a **change order history list** under the panel with per-doc PDF links.
- ⚠️ **The inertness guard caught the PDF route as a second reader — working as designed.** It can't go through `lib/co-docs`: it runs as the **service role**, which bypasses RLS, while `lib/co-docs` relies on it. Allowlisted **with its reason**, and it carries its own org check against `co_docs.org_id` — **that check IS the security, there is no policy behind it.**
- **`scripts/verify-co-doc-pdf.mjs` renders the PDF FOR REAL** (10 checks: Pajot's three items, the countersigned copy, a credit-only doc, every optional field absent at once, and a 25-item doc that crosses a page break). ⚠️ **@react-pdf throws at RENDER time, not compile time — `tsc` proves nothing about a PDF.** Needed an explicit `import React` on the component: Next compiles JSX with the automatic runtime and `tsx` with the classic one, so without it the only way to learn the PDF crashes is a client asking for it.

**✅ 2026-09-15: STEP 2 IS BUILT (`3d037f7`) — but NOT the way the spec described, and the data is why.**
- ⛔ **"REOPEN THE SUB IN THE COMPOSER" IS IMPOSSIBLE ON THE JOBS THIS IS FOR.** `AddLineComposer` refuses any line without `product_key`, and the Built importer writes frozen lines with dept hours + a material lump and **no `product_key` at all**. Measured with **`scripts/inspect-line-editability`**: **Pajot 0/7 · Batia 0/9 · Leonard 0/7 and 0/8 · Schiller 0/4 · Bonzer 0/15 — every imported job is 0%.** 14 of 64 lines across all sold jobs (22%), and the editable ones are native jobs and test rows.
- **So `edit_sub` is a LINE-LEVEL DIFF:** `removeLineIds` (credited at contract value) · `addLines` (charged at today's rates) · `reviseLines` for the minority that ARE composer lines. **That is the spec's own money rule — "a modification = credit old + add new" — made literal, and it is exactly what Pajot's change is** (remove the rounded end panel, add a finish panel, add a waterfall top).
- ⛔ **A FROZEN SUB CANNOT TAKE NEW LINES, and this is the trap worth remembering.** Its stored costs ARE its price (108), so appending a composer line prices that line at material cost with no labor and no margin — **108's bug, one level down.** The freeze is per-SUBPROJECT; the need would be per-LINE. Rather than move the freeze onto the line (a third migration on the hot pricing path), **new work on a frozen sub goes on the change order as its own scope**, which already prices correctly. Removing/crediting from a frozen sub is unaffected, and that IS the common case. Gated in `co-doc-math` (pure, verified), **re-checked in `saveEditDraft`**, and explained in the modal rather than silently disabled.
- **Revisions UPDATE the line in place** at acceptance rather than delete+insert, so it keeps its id and anything hanging off it survives.
- **The PDF lists what a revision REMOVED as well as what it added** — showing only additions makes the credit read as an unexplained discount.
- `canAddLinesToSub` moved to `co-doc-math` **so it could be verified at all** — it's pure, and it was sitting behind `lib/supabase` where no verify script could reach it. Same trap as `formatThickness`.

**✅ 2026-09-15: STEP 3 IS BUILT (`9e00830`) · ⛔ MIGRATION `109` NEEDS TO RUN ON PROD.** `cash_flow_receivables.co_doc_id` — the v2 doc could NOT reuse `change_order_id` (it FKs to `change_orders`), so a parallel column with the same three properties 106 was built for: identity, idempotency (partial unique index — acceptance isn't a transaction and a doc can be re-accepted), and protection from re-import.
- ⛔ **ONE INVOICE PER DOCUMENT — the OPPOSITE of v1.** v1 appends every CO to a single ROLLING invoice per project, which is exactly why *"what is this $4,890 for?"* had no answer. A v2 doc is already the unit the client signs, so it's the unit they're billed for. Idempotent through `co_docs.qbo_invoice_id`. ⚠️ This writes the **MillSuite** invoice; the QuickBooks push stays operator-initiated, same as the contract flow.
- ⛔ **NET CREDITS RAISE NOTHING** — no invoice, no draw. You don't bill for money you owe back, and a negative line on a client-facing schedule reads as a mistake. **The acceptance message now says that happened**, or it looks like the billing silently failed.
- The draw keeps every rule `appendCoDrawRow` paid for: positive only · **no schedule ⇒ no row** (writing one would pull the project out of the "No payment schedule" tray while the rest of its contract stayed unscheduled) · **must sort LAST** via `coDrawSlot` or it soaks up payments belonging to earlier draws.
- ⛔ **THE RE-IMPORT GUARD HAD TO LEARN ABOUT v2.** The Built importer and `saveMilestones` both clear `status='projected'` receivables and re-insert; that already destroyed a v1 CO draw once (`3c75674`). Guarding `change_order_id` but not `co_doc_id` would leave **the identical hole, exactly the width of the new feature.** Both now guard both.
- ⚠️ **AND THE FALLBACK IS STEPWISE, which is the subtle part.** PostgREST fails the WHOLE statement on one unknown column, so on a pre-109 database asking for `co_doc_id` errors — and dropping straight to "no guard" would delete the v1 draws that 106 protects. It retries **both → v1-only → none**.
- ⚠️ **The importer's delete never checked its error.** Pre-109 it would have silently no-opped and then inserted a **second full schedule on top of the first**. It now throws instead of double-writing a payment schedule.

**✅ 2026-09-15: STEP 4 IS BUILT (`27dfa5a`) — CHANGE ORDERS V2 IS FEATURE-COMPLETE. ✅ MIGRATION `110` IS ON PROD** (`co_docs.sent_at`, `signed_ip`, `signed_pdf_url`) — verified in the schema cache.
- ⛔ **THE PORTAL RECORDS CONSENT; THE APP APPLIES THE MONEY.** The spec said acceptance could come from *"an in-app mark OR the portal signature"*. Taken literally that puts `acceptDoc` — materialise subprojects, rewrite lines, recompute `bid_total`, raise an invoice, write a draw — behind an **unauthenticated URL**. It can't even run there: `lib/co-docs` uses the BROWSER client, RLS refuses every write in a route handler, and **PostgREST reports a refusal as a ZERO-ROW SUCCESS**, so a half-applied acceptance would return `ok`. So the doc stays open carrying the signature, the panel says **"Signed by X — still needs your Accept to apply it"**, and one code path moves the contract total. Same split as v1, for reasons that are *stronger* here because v2 acceptance does far more.
- ⛔ **`sent_at` IS A SAFETY GATE, NOT BOOKKEEPING.** "Open" means the shop is still composing — drafts coming and going, prices moving. Without it every open doc would appear in the client portal **with a signature box under it**. "Send to client" sets it; the portal reads *sent, not yet accepted*.
- `signed_pdf_url` is separate from `pdf_url` because writing the countersigned copy over the same path **destroys the blank-signature PDF the shop already emailed** — v1 learned that one.
- ⛔ **STEP 3 HAD LEFT A HOLE HERE, FOUND ON THE WAY.** `findContractInvoice` and its service-role twin in `lib/client-portal` pick the contract invoice by excluding **v1** CO invoices. v2 docs now raise their own — so on a project whose first invoice is a change order's, **the CO would have been shown to the CLIENT as their contract invoice**, and `ensureContractInvoice` would have appended contract lines to it. Both now exclude `co_docs.qbo_invoice_id`.
- ⚠️ **THE NEW GUARD FAILED TO FAIL — THIRD TIME TODAY.** `verify-co-docs` now asserts the portal read gates on `sent_at`. The first version matched `sent_at` **anywhere** after `from('co_docs')` and passed with the filter deleted, because the column also appears in the SELECT list. It now requires an actual `.not()`/`.is()` filter, **verified by deleting the gate and watching it go red.** A check that cannot fail looks exactly like a check that passes.

**A. ⛔ SCHILLER WAS NEVER REWRITTEN. NOTHING WAS DELETED.** The inspector shows all three rows intact — **50% $24,538 · 25% $12,269 · 25% $12,269**, created `2026-09-03 20:24:42` at import, never modified. **The bug was the DISPLAY:** `buildPaymentsView` dropped any draw with nothing outstanding, so once the last payment landed the whole schedule vanished from the board and left only five receipt cards (four ~$12k + a $2), which reads exactly like "25/25/25/25 with the deposit deleted". The $2 was Andrew's own true-up, not an orphan.
- **Fixed:** settled draws now stay in their month — greyed, "paid in full", excluded from every total. The project ledger also shows the schedule read-only with each draw's state, reconciled through the same `reconcileProject` the board uses. **Post-sale there had been NO surface showing the agreed terms at all.**
- **⛔ A REAL LATENT HOLE WAS FOUND AND CLOSED ON THE WAY (`83c9ab6`), just not this incident's cause.** `saveMilestones` deletes `status='projected'` rows, and its comment claimed that "can't clobber real payments" — true only under the OLD model. **Payments v2 made paid-ness DERIVED: `logPayment` never touches the draw, so a fully-paid draw is still `status='projected'` and was deletable.** A guard silently disarmed by a later refactor while still reading as correct. **The guard is now CASH, not status** — a project with any recorded payment refuses a wholesale rewrite, enforced in BOTH deleting paths (`saveMilestones` and the Built importer's milestone step), and the refusal is surfaced instead of returning a silent `false`.
- **⛔ THE ACTUAL MONEY BUG: every generated schedule was $1 off.** `Math.round(total × pct / 100)` per row, so 50% of $49,075 → $24,538 and each 25% → $12,269 = **$49,076**. That is where the phantom dollars and the correcting payments came from. Both generators now use `allocateRounded` (largest remainder, already in `lib/allocate` and previously used only for display). **✅ REPAIRED ON PROD 2026-09-15** via `scripts/fix-draw-rounding.mjs --apply` — Schiller, Hunt, Murtagh. Re-run reports 0 changes; **Schiller now foots exactly: draws $49,075 = cash $49,075.**

**B + C. ✅ YOU CAN ADD DRAWS AGAIN.** Andrew: *"I cant add draws."* A sold project with no schedule had no reachable way to get one — the tray linked to the project page, whose only route was the milestone builder, which dead-ended on imported jobs. **The tray card now opens a Set-up-draws modal ON THE BOARD**: label + percent rows, presets, add/remove, live total, **Save gated on exactly 100%**, dollars derived through `allocateRounded`. It needs nothing but `bid_total`, so an imported job behaves like a native one. `createDrawSchedule` **REFUSES if draws already exist** — it creates, never replaces.

An entry clocked at 8pm Eastern bucketed into tomorrow, and **Friday evening's hours landed on a Saturday the view doesn't render, so they vanished.** Found because the new banner would have disagreed with the columns beneath it.

**1. ✅ `/time`** — every entry shows who tracked it; search + project/person dropdowns + date + **Today · This week · Last week** chips. **⛔ THE FETCH WINDOW WENT 7 DAYS → 30**: "Last week" reaches 13 days back, so on a 7-day fetch that chip would have returned nothing and looked broken when the data simply wasn't loaded. `verify-time-filters` asserts that coupling rather than leaving it to memory. Filtering is on `started_at` (falling back to `created_at`) — **a manual entry for last Tuesday is CREATED today**, and filtering on the wrong one files back-dated hours in the wrong week. Chip weeks are **Mon..SUNDAY** (a Mon–Fri filter would hide Saturday's entries with no explanation). Dropdowns list the ORG's projects and roster, not values present in the entries — picking someone who logged nothing should say "nothing matches", not hide the person you wanted to check on.

**3. ✅ Divide-block modal** — weeks is a **typed number capped at 52** (committed on blur, so typing "12" doesn't pass through "1" and collapse the rows); **moving the first week walks the rest along** at +7 days; **custom mode** once any hours or any non-first date is edited, with an explicit "Split evenly again" to opt back in. Changing the count while custom **resizes** the list instead of regenerating it.

So this table was never checked, and **not being checked looked exactly like passing**. 083 locked down what the audit flagged; `100` caught two tables skipped while EMPTY; this one was never listed at all. Two ways to be invisible, same outcome.
- **The audit now reads every `CREATE TABLE` out of `db/migrations` and refuses a clean run while any is unlisted.** That surfaced **34 never-audited tables** in one go — `department_allocations` was the only one actually exposed; `team_compensation` (29), `qb_connections` (1), `stripe_events` (2), `project_events` (24) and `department_members` (87) all came back correctly blocked. ⚠️ **A table created OUTSIDE a migration is still invisible to this check** — and `department_allocations` proves that happens: prod has an `org_id` column migration 001 never mentions.

**✅ 2026-09-12: PAYMENTS BOARD — TWO FIXES FROM ANDREW'S LIVE LOOK (`406a2fe`).**
- **⛔ "NEEDED" MEANT TWO DIFFERENT THINGS ON ONE PAGE.** The month card summed its scheduled draws and called that "needed" — **the exact definition the sales goal replaced** — while the banner above uses "needed" for the target. Now: a month that has happened shows what **LANDED**, a month ahead shows what's **PLANNED**. September reads `RECEIVED $49,075 · $70,974 still scheduled`; October reads `SCHEDULED $63,839`. **The "still scheduled" tail is load-bearing** — without it a live month hides the gap between plan and reality. `isNow` is deliberately NOT future: money can land in the month you're standing in.
- **⛔ THE PAGER IS STICKY, AND THAT'S THE ACTUAL FIX.** Andrew: "the arrows are at the top of the page and i have to scroll up to advance." **Moving it above the grid alone would NOT have worked** — September's column is taller than the viewport, so it would still scroll away. `sticky top-14` clears the app nav (`h-14`); ⚠️ **raise the nav and you must raise this.** Label compacts to "Sep – Nov 2026". Paging away now says the goal banner still reads this month, so the two totals can't look like they disagree.

**✅ 2026-09-12: THE PRODUCTION PROGRESS BAR WAS COUNTING THE WRONG HOURS — FIXED (`45dec92`), CONFIRMED LIVE BY ANDREW.** He reported it as "not working anymore… i thought we built this already." It WAS built (wave-3 item 4, `1ae267b`) and the component was fine; two things around it were not.
- **⛔ IT READ SUBPROJECT-SUMMED TIME.** The page fed it `proj.actualMinutes`, accumulated from `loadSubprojectActualHours`, which queries `.in('subproject_id', …)` and **skips any row whose `subproject_id` is null**. But clocking in only requires a **PROJECT** — `/time` gates its button on `timerProjectId` alone and inserts `subproject_id: timerSubprojectId || null`; manual entry needs only a project and hours. **So every hour logged without picking a sub was invisible, and the bar sat at 0% while time was being tracked.** `/projects` has always used `loadProjectActuals` and showed the bigger number, so **the two pages already disagreed.** The strip and the Hours section now read one project-level source.
- **⚠️ The per-dept rows still won't always sum to the total** — time clocked with no department belongs to no row. That's the rule the subproject path already followed (`actualUnmappedMinutes`), not a new bug.
- **⛔ AND THE TWO EMPTY STATES WERE INDISTINGUISHABLE:** "no estimate" and "nothing tracked yet" both drew a plain gray connector, which is most of why this read as broken rather than as having nothing to say. An unestimated job in production now carries its own tooltip.
- `scripts/verify-production-bar.mjs` pins the arithmetic + state machine (25 checks), including teeth-checks that the two actuals sources differ and that the two empty states stay tellable apart.
- **⚠️ Worth knowing: there were only 3 `time_entries` rows in the whole database** when this was diagnosed. If the bar looks quiet on a job, check whether anyone has actually clocked to it before suspecting code.

**⛔ 2026-09-04: THE STALENESS BANNER WAS FIRING ON LINES THAT CANNOT BE RECOMPUTED — FIXED (`a951d97`).** Andrew: "pops up randomly… doesn't seem like it makes any sense." **`computeBreakdown` resolves every slot with `find() || null` and prices a null as ZERO**, so an id that stops resolving (archived material, deleted door type) doesn't error — the line recomputes far cheaper, trips the threshold, and flags a line nobody touched.
- **⚠️ THE BANNER WAS THE SYMPTOM; THE HAZARD IS THE REFRESH.** "Update to latest rates" WRITES the recomputed numbers back, so refreshing one of these would have **banked the zero and deleted real material cost from a live estimate**. Same shape as the imported re-pricing bug.
- **`unresolvedSlotIds` (lib/composer) checks all nine id-bearing slots**; `checkLineStaleness` skips any line with an unresolved id and logs which. **Those lines aren't stale, they're UNRESOLVABLE — the fix is re-picking the slot, not recomputing.** Same guard for a door type or drawer style that resolves but is **uncalibrated**, which zeroes labour the same way (the breakdown already flagged it; nothing acted on the flag).
- **Ruled out, don't re-investigate:** a shop-rate change *cannot* cause this. `materialSubtotal` is purely material — no labour — so `lump_cost_override` doesn't move when the rate does. The module claimed it; now it's checked.
- `scripts/verify-composer-staleness.mjs` pins it, including that **all nine slots are covered** — an unchecked slot is one that can silently price at zero.

Nothing blocking — Andrew's live pass is the only thing left. Detail in Now.

**⛔ 2026-09-04: THE HANDOFF SCREEN WAS RE-PRICING IMPORTED JOBS — FIXED (`08d2169`), and it was one click from corrupting a contract.** Andrew caught it before selling Bonzer: handoff showed **$257,907** against the project page's **$168,090** on a frozen 6c-2 job. It built its pricing context from the live org rate + org consumables with **no imported handling and no locked-rate handling**, so the job paid for its labour TWICE — once inside the frozen line price Built already quoted, once as hours × the current org rate — plus consumables.
- **NOT cosmetic. `handleConfirm` writes `projectTotals.total` into `projects.bid_total` and THEN flips the stage**, so confirming would have overwritten a real contract, locked the estimate against the wrong number, and fed every downstream surface from it.
- **Both rules now live in `lib/pricing`: `effectiveShopRate` + `effectiveConsumablePct`** (imported ⇒ 0; otherwise a locked rate beats the org rate). The project page reads them too. ⛔ **Don't rebuild either rule at a call site.**
- Margins were NOT involved — the importer pins them to 0 and `resolveBucketMargins` uses `??`, so an explicit 0 survives. Worth knowing before suspecting margin next time.
- ⚠️ **The handoff page's local `Project` interface didn't even declare `imported_at` / `locked_shop_rate`.** The row loads with `select('*')` so the data was always there — just invisible to the type. **A page that prices money must type the fields that decide the price.**
- **THIRD surface to re-price a frozen job** (after the subproject page, `9883b46`). If a new surface computes a project total, check it against an imported job before shipping.

**⚠️ SOLID WOOD TOP PRICES CHANGED 2026-09-01 (`e3efbc4`) — they go UP, and that's the fix.** Two bugs Andrew caught: BdFt was measured on the TYPED finished thickness instead of the rough stock it's milled from (a 1.5" top out of 8/4 eats 2" — his 40×24 read 10 BdFt against a real 13.33, under-counting material AND the labor that scales off it), and waste was charged twice (the component's % and then the per-line knob on the inflated subtotal). **A staleness refresh will reprice saved Solid Wood Top lines upward — expected, not a regression.** ⛔ **An existing Solid Wood Top calibration entered as a FINISHED thickness must be re-entered as ROUGH**, or labor now scales high: lines and the calibration must share the rough basis.

**⚠️ PRICES CHANGED 2026-09-01 (`40d67c9`) — expect totals to go UP on any project with freeform/vendor lines.** Those lines (`unit_price_override`) were contributing **$0** to every rollup: subproject panel, project `priceTotal`, kanban, sales cards. The estimate PDF sums saved line totals and always included them, so the app had been disagreeing with its own estimates. Fixed via a `customCost` bucket. **Anything already quoted from the app rather than the PDF was under-priced** — worth a look at open bids.

**⚠️ /schedule NOW HAS TWO WEEK COORDINATE SYSTEMS — read the block at the top of `page.tsx` before touching week math.** ABSOLUTE (week 0 = this Monday) holds `blocks`, undo history, the AI what-if path and `weekIndexToDate`; VIEW (column 0 = leftmost visible) holds everything rendered. `displayBlocks` is the only bridge. Paging moves the window, not the data — which is why an in-flight what-if and the undo stack survive it. Wiring `weekIndexToDate` to the display origin would silently rewrite every dragged allocation by however far the operator had paged.

**✅ NEW 2026-08-31: CLIENT PORTAL — BUILT, MIGRATION `092` RUN ON PROD, AND DEPLOYED.** (`96dcb4e` migration · `af73483` read model + the two writes · `ed9bd56` pages · `bea7728` view split + layout fixes · `e5c7799` the shop's side · `feaa449` design files.) tsc clean, production `next build` clean (49 static pages; both portal routes correctly `ƒ` server-rendered, never static), `check-tour-targets` PASS, both screens rendered and read at 375px and 1280px against fixture data. **Deploy verified live 2026-08-31:** `x-matched-path: /portal/[token]` on prod, so the route is matched rather than merely 404-ing as a nonexistent path; `/api/portal-admin/link` → 401 unauthenticated; both write routes → 400 on an empty body and a uniform **404** for an unknown *and* a malformed token, with no 500 and nothing to tell the two apart. **Nothing blocking — only Andrew's live pass**, list at the end of "### Client portal" in Now. Both open calls were answered by Andrew 2026-08-31 and are closed.

**⛔ THE PORTAL LINK'S HOST HAS BROKEN TWICE. Both traps are written into `baseUrl()` in `/api/portal-admin/link` — read them before touching it.** This is the URL the shop texts to a client, so a wrong host isn't cosmetic.
- **Trap 1 — a hand-set constant rots.** It first read `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL || NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`. `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` is set on prod to **`app.millsuite.com`, which has no DNS record**, so the first link Andrew copied was `DNS_PROBE_FINISHED_NXDOMAIN`. This route was the **only** consumer of either variable in the whole codebase, so the stale value had no other symptom — nothing could catch it before a human pasted the link into a browser. **Don't resurrect a manually configured URL here.**
- **Trap 2 — the request host is whatever the SHOP is browsing.** Deriving purely from the request (`d25540c`) was correct and still unusable: on Vercel that's often a per-deployment URL, so the link came out on `millsuite-mvp-profit-3yvwt9k3q-andrew-watsons-projects-4a21bd73.vercel.app` — a dead 22-character host swapped for a live 74-character one.
- **Now (`d9d5bd8`):** on production use `VERCEL_PROJECT_PRODUCTION_URL` (the platform injects the project's real production domain, so it can't drift the way a hand-set var did); everywhere else fall back to the request host, because on a preview deploy the point is to test *that* deployment. **If a copied link ever shows a `vercel.app` host again, that env var isn't populated** — check it before changing code, and note the fallback is working as designed.
- **Length was Andrew's call 2026-08-31: leave it.** The copied link is the HOME link (`/portal/{token}`, ~65 chars incl. the domain) — the long URL he first hit was a project page he'd navigated to, not something the button produces. A shorter token, a `/p/` prefix and a dedicated short domain were all offered and declined. (Canonical host is `www.millsuite.com`; the apex 307s to it.)

**⛔ PORTAL PAYMENTS: A MILESTONE MARKED RECEIVED DOES NOT ALWAYS TOUCH THE INVOICE. Know this before changing `loadPayments`.** `lib/milestones.markMilestoneReceived` behaves differently by invoicing mode: **internal** mode flips the milestone AND records a payment on the contract invoice; **QuickBooks** mode flips the milestone and deliberately stops, because money is meant to arrive via the QB watcher, so `client_invoices.amount_received` legitimately stays 0. **Built is a QB org.** The portal's first version read only invoice payments *and* filtered received milestones out of the schedule, so Andrew's project with real money against it rendered **"$0 paid of $26,227" with the paid rows missing entirely** — the only record that existed was the one thing being ignored. Fixed 2026-08-31 (`cd1d47c`): every milestone renders carrying its own status, and `paid = Math.max(invoicePayments, receivedMilestones)`. **MAX, not sum** — internal mode records the same money in both places, so adding them doubles the figure on a client-facing page.

**Portal layout follows the content, on purpose (`cd1d47c`).** A project with no photos, approvals or change orders has nothing for a second column, and the two-column grid turned that into a ragged band of cards with two thirds of the screen empty underneath. `rich = photos || approvals || changeOrders` picks between one narrow centred column and the two-column layout; the home screen does the same for a single project. **Most projects are sparse early on, so the sparse path is the common case, not an edge case** — check it first if you change the layout. Also from that pass: the header is full-bleed (it's chrome, not a floating card), the org logo is `object-contain` in an auto-width box (a square `object-cover` cropped Built's wordmark to "bui"), and shop photos are plain links to the full image (no lightbox — works with no JS, pinch-zooms on a phone, can be saved).

**✅ Incidental but important: migration `092` is confirmed live on prod.** The broken link still carried a real 32-char token (`HTd_hQXzW4B3hWmPYy3bn2L4rkbUYGDB`), which means `clients.portal_token` exists, the service-role write succeeded and the row came back — the end-to-end schema check the unauthenticated probes couldn't reach.

**⛔ THE PORTAL RECORDS CONSENT; THE APP APPLIES THE MONEY. Don't "finish the job" by making the portal flip a change order to `approved`.** `approveCo()` chains into `applyApprovedCo` → `recomputeProjectBidTotal`, all on the **browser** supabase client — which in a route handler has no session, so RLS refuses it and PostgREST reports the refusal as a **zero-row success**. Re-implementing that on a public endpoint, first exercised by a real client, isn't a trade worth making. So portal signing stamps `signed_name`/`signed_at`/`signed_pdf_url` and leaves the CO in `sent_to_client`; the CO card then tells the shop to approve it, which runs the one path that has always moved the contract total. The same reasoning drives the approve guard: an approval item carrying a **draft** CO is deliberately not one-click approvable in the portal, because approving it in-app finalizes a priced change the client was never shown a number for.

**⛔ `lib/client-portal.ts` IS A SECURITY BOUNDARY, NOT AN ORDINARY LIB.** It runs on the service role and is reachable by anyone holding a URL. Every select in it names its columns by hand; there is **no `select('*')` and there must never be one** — these tables carry costs, margins, labor hours and other clients' rows. Adding a field to a portal type means adding the column deliberately and asking whether a client should see it. The portal pages are React **Server** Components on purpose: the browser never holds a Supabase key there, and there is no public GET endpoint to enumerate. Only the two writes are routes.

**✅ "Small fixes wave" — ALL 10 ITEMS BUILT 2026-08-27** (`6d16258` project rename · `d67230a` materials-catalog organization · `d880979` collapsible subproject scope · `fb85606` drawer edit-modal truth-up · `8359a18` finishes-tab truth-up · `a474c25` interior-finishes naming · `a9abbfc` flat interior-finish set · `04cb629` subproject rename · `8aeccab` estimate closing note · `51e1fec` estimate line readability · `e771ddc` PDF glyph sanitizer). **Items 1–3 + 6 confirmed working live by Andrew.** tsc clean throughout, every touched route compiles 200, `check-tour-targets` passes (44 targets, no duplicates). **Migrations `090` + `091` ✅ BOTH RUN ON PROD 2026-08-27 by Andrew** (091 verified through PostgREST with the public key: `select=id,estimate_closing_note&limit=0` → 200, so the column is in the schema cache). **Nothing blocking — deploy, then Andrew's live pass on 4, 5, 7, 8, 9, 10 + the glyph check.** The guide-v2 live QA list stays parked, not dropped; no new walkthrough work until then.

**⛔ PDF TEXT MUST GO THROUGH `pdfText` (`lib/pdf-text.ts`) — ✅ FIXED 2026-08-27 (`e771ddc`), and this is the rule going forward.** The PDFs use the built-in Helvetica, which react-pdf encodes as **WinAnsi**. A character outside that set does NOT error — it renders as a different glyph or vanishes. `8′ ceiling height` was printing as **`82 ceiling height`** on client-facing estimates: a silently corrupted *number*.
- **Broken (replaced):** `′`→`'` · `″`→`"` · `−`, `‐`, `‑` → `-` · `⁄`→`/` · `→`→` to `. **Fine, left alone:** en dash, em dash, curly quotes, ellipsis, `°`, `×`, `½`, `•`. Every entry was rendered through the real component and eyeballed — **don't add a replacement without doing the same**, since several risky-looking characters are fine and rewriting them makes output worse.
- The map had been reinvented **three times** (`InvoicePdf.pdfText`, `ChangeOrderPdf.pdfSafe`, and nothing at all in `EstimatePdf` — which is why the prime bug lived there). Now one lib; both old copies also rewrote en/em dashes needlessly, which is gone. **New PDF surface ⇒ wrap dynamic strings in `pdfText`.**

**⚠️ Item 7 ships a self-healing DATA RENAME — know this before debugging a "finish item changed name" report.** Opening `/rate-book` (or the finish walkthrough) folds legacy door-style finish names onto the flat set: "Paint on shaker"→"Paint", "Paint on slab"→"Gloss paint", "Stain + clear on shaker"→"Stain + clear", "Stain + clear on slab"→"Clear". It's guarded (uncalibrated rows only, target name must be free) and idempotent, but it IS an automatic write to `rate_book_items.name`. `renameLegacyFinishCombos` in `lib/finish-breakdown.ts`.

**⛔ `subprojects` HAS NO `updated_at` COLUMN** (`projects` does). **⛔ NEVER WRITE TO `users` FROM THE BROWSER.** `users` has RLS on with a SELECT-self policy and a DELETE policy and **no UPDATE policy**, so `supabase.from('users').update(...)` matches zero rows and PostgREST returns `{ error: null }` — success-shaped silence that `if (error) throw` cannot catch. This had been quietly breaking `onboarded_at` since 083 landed on prod (2026-08-06); found and fixed 2026-08-12. **⛔ GOTCHA THAT COST A WHOLE SESSION — know this before debugging anything data-related.** **Next.js 14 caches database READS made in API routes** (it patches global `fetch`; supabase-js reads through it). Writes aren't cached, so a bug here looks exactly like "saving is broken" when saving is perfect. Fixed app-wide 2026-08-06 (`6766014`) via a `cache: 'no-store'` fetch in `lib/supabase-admin.ts` + `lib/supabase.ts` — **keep it there**, and never `createClient` a service-role client inline. `dynamic = 'force-dynamic'` does NOT cover it. ## Where things stand

MillSuite is live and in use. Estimating (rate book, composer, project rollup), scheduling, capacity calendar, invoicing, and Stripe billing are all shipped. Two beta testers are on it.

**Estimates + invoices rebuild — DONE & live (2026-06-19).** Migrations **057–061** run on prod; all commits pushed to `main`. The model now:
- **Estimates** live in MillSuite, delivered as a **branded PDF** ("Send estimate" → download + copy-email; no email infra). Never pushed to QB. (`components/estimates/EstimatePdf.tsx` + `app/api/estimates/[projectId]/pdf` + `SendEstimateModal`.)
- **One invoice per project = the contract** (`bid_total`, estimate line items, milestones as a payment-terms schedule). QB mode pushes it to QB **and** records a matching MillSuite invoice (`client_invoices.qbo_invoice_id` link); internal mode creates it in MillSuite. **Draws** (any amount) lower the balance; the **QB watcher** (`lib/qb-events.ts`) applies incoming payments to the invoice. **Milestones are projection-only** (no longer flip to "received"); dashboard AR reads the invoice balance.
- **QB OAuth/connect** (per-org tokens) live — Andrew connected to Built LLC. Estimate→QB push retired; per-milestone invoicing replaced by the one invoice.

Remaining from this item: **Change Orders** — now **parked** (see "Parked — resume later").

**Top nav shipped (2026-06-19).** Replaced the top bar with a **hoisted** nav — `components/top-nav.tsx`, rendered once in `app/(app)/layout.tsx`; **text-only (no icons)**, 3 grouped dropdowns: **Sales** (click → /sales; hover → Kanban/Invoices/Clients) · **Projects** (click → /projects; hover → Schedule/Capacity) · **Manage** (dropdown-only: Reports/Suggestions/Rate book/Team/Time). Same `hasAccess` gating; member→Time; Invoices plan-gated only. (We tried a slide-out drawer first — Andrew preferred the top bar.) **Cleanup done (2026-06-22):** removed the `import Nav`/`<Nav/>` from all 20 pages and deleted the `components/nav.tsx` stub — nav now lives solely in `app/(app)/layout.tsx` via `components/top-nav.tsx`. (tsc clean; grep for `@/components/nav`/`<Nav/>` under `app/` empty.)

**Projects dashboard + production lifecycle — shipped 2026-06-23.** No schema change. **(A) Lifecycle:** `sold` relabeled **"Pre-Production"**, `production` → **"In Production"**; "Ready for production" is a **derived** sub-state (not a stored stage). Auto-advance is gone — `lib/project-stage.ts` now exposes `isReadyForProduction()` (read-only gate) + `startProduction()` (the sole writer: flips stage + seeds allocations). Production starts **manually** via a readiness-gated "Start production" button + a green Ready banner on the project page; the status-bar Pre-Production pip green-checks when ready. **Deposit signal resolved:** the contract invoice's `amount_received > 0` (post-rebuild, milestones only flip to "received" on full payment, so the old milestone gate was dead). **(B) `/projects` dashboard:** rebuilt as the post-sold view — 5 derived buckets (Pre-production / Ready / In production / Installed / Complete) + client/project search + 3 filter-driven metrics (Value / Est hrs / Tracked hrs). Commits `317d9c9` (A) + `1df7eef` (B). _Made it the main `/projects` index (the open decision). Dropped the old per-card delete (not in the prototype) — say if you want it back. Per-project hours load is N parallel calls (fine at beta scale). End-to-end QA on a real sold project still pending._

**Capacity calendar redesign — shipped 2026-07-16.** `/capacity` is now a **standalone birdseye planning tool**, no longer a mirror of `/schedule`. All in `app/(app)/capacity/page.tsx`; the side pane is unchanged. 5 commits on `main`:
- **Cut the schedule connection fully** (`edd02e7`) — deleted `lib/capacity-seed.ts`; removed every `autoSeedProjectMonthAllocations`/`triggerAutoSeedCapacity` call from `schedule/page.tsx` + `lib/schedule-seed.ts`; stripped the `source` auto/manual concept (auto badge, "Pin to this month" + `pinAllocation`, all `source:'manual'` writes). The `source` DB column stays (defaults `'manual'`, no migration); all rows read as manual.
- **Rolling 12-month window + layout rebuild** (`a940991`) — replaced the year-picker + quarter/half/year zoom with a rolling window (current month + next 11, arrows page ±1, `Today` jump; crosses the year boundary). Fixed-width 184px month columns in one horizontal scroll row; unscheduled tray moved **above** the months (drop-to-unschedule); 12-cell util heat strip (click scrolls a month into view); header stats (Next opening + lead time / Booked / Staffing signal); month card keeps the tiny 3px dept-stacked bar, the 6-row per-dept mini-bar block deleted; ProjectCard collapsed from 3 zoom variants to one.
- **Quiet PTO/holiday line** (`56c3e7e`) — replaced the 🏛/🏖 chips + per-day flag strip with one muted "N holidays · N PTO days" line (dates + names in the tooltip); deleted `MonthOverrideFlags`.
- **Pipeline toggle** (`2b461fb`) — "Sold only" (default) vs "Sold + pipeline". Pipeline (`new_lead`/`fifty_fifty`/`ninety_percent`) cards render dashed + a stage badge; their hours fold into utilization/bars/lead-time/staffing (and a lighter 2nd bar segment) **only when the toggle is on** — off = hidden but persisted. "Booked" stays sold-only in both modes.

Verified: tsc clean; grep `capacity-seed|autoSeed|source.*auto|pinAllocation` under `app/`+`lib/` empty; `/capacity` compiles (200). _**Left for Andrew: interactive QA in the logged-in app** (preview can't auth) — drag sold + pipeline on/off months, confirm the toggle flips the math, the rolling window crosses Dec→Jan, and Even + Smart splits still work (covers the outstanding Smart-split QA)._

**Capacity 0h-capacity bug — fixed 2026-07-17** (`95135dd`). Every month read **0h capacity** because `/capacity` still queried the legacy dept-members join table, which `/team` stopped writing in the shop-rate PR — assignments now live on `team_members.dept_assignments` (jsonb), the source `/schedule` already reads. Fix: derive per-dept billable headcount from `loadShopRateSetup().team` (billable members whose `dept_assignments` include the dept), dropping the dept-members query + `deptMembers` state. Hardened the zero-capacity case so cap=0 never reads as a healthy 0%: a month with hours but no capacity is treated as "over" (heat red, staffing counts it, next-opening skips it, bar clamps to 100%); an empty month reads neutral gray, not green; and a whole window with no capacity shows a warning banner (→ /team, page not gated) + a "No team capacity set up" staffing signal. tsc clean; grep `department_members` under `app/(app)/capacity/` empty; `/capacity` compiles (200). _Still wants Andrew's live-app confirm that real numbers show (≈ billable × 8h × ~21d per dept)._

**Team + shop rate + worker time app — built 2026-07-17 (chunks A–D).** All tsc-clean; every route compiles (200). 8 commits on `main`:
- **A1 member depth + per-person hours** (`b8eca02`) — extended `orgs.team_members` jsonb (email/phone/title/start_date/hours_per_week/active, all backfilled) + /team edit pane. Per-person `hours_per_week` now drives the shop-rate denominator (`sumBillableHoursYear`) and per-dept capacity (`deptDailyHoursByTeam` = Σ hours_per_week/5); `/capacity` + `/schedule` read it in place of headcount × dept.hours_per_day (40h/wk = 8h/day, so seeded depts unchanged). Inactive members drop out of both. _The onboarding walkthrough + settings still show the uniform `computeBillableHoursYear` figure (equal at default) — fold to per-person later._
- **A2 accounts** (`14021d6`) — owner-only `POST /api/admin/users` (service-role, Bearer-verified, org-scoped): create_login (seat-checked, creates auth user + users row, rollback on failure) / reset_password / unlink. /team AccountControls write the returned `user_id` onto `team_members` — the one explicit roster→login bridge.
- **A3 role landing** (`befc515`) — RoleGate confines members to `/me` (was `/time`); top-nav "My work" → /me; minimal `/me` skeleton (D filled it).
- **B PTO** (`74e1e3a`) — **migration `062_pto.sql` (idempotent) MUST run on prod before deploy** (pto_requests + pto_policies, org-scoped RLS). `lib/pto.ts` + /team Time-off section (approve/deny queue, tenure-band policy editor, per-member balance bar). Approve writes one `capacity_overrides` row per weekday (hours_reduction = member's daily hours); deny/delete clears them. /team load is guarded so it renders even if 062 hasn't run.
- **C shop-rate extras** (`7ac13cb`) — no migration (reuses `shop_rate_snapshots` from 001). /team margin ladder (break-even + 15/20/25/30%), snapshot on "Save…", margin alert strip (active jobs under break-even × 1.15).
