## Now

### Demo completed-projects seed for marketing screenshots — ✅ SEEDED 2026-09-16 · ⚠️ Andrew's eyeball + Cowork screenshots remaining

**Why:** the marketing site's proof section needs a real screenshot of Reports → Completed Projects (list + expanded diagnostic drawer + margin alerts) and Built's org has zero completed projects. Brief: `docs/marketing-site-demo-data-brief.md`.

**Andrew's calls (2026-09-16):** seed a **dedicated demo org, "Bayside Millworks"** — NOT Built's org, despite the `demo_seed.sql` precedent at repo root that targeted Built with fixed UUIDs (inspect confirmed it was never applied: 0 rows).

**✅ SEEDED (Andrew ran it; every self-check green):** org `36f655a7-b989-4073-bb9d-3c5585623738` · slug `bayside-millworks` · owner `andrew+demo@builtthings.com` (temp password with Andrew) · plan internal. 7 clients / 7 projects / 12 subprojects / 12 lines / 22 receivables / 246 time entries / 7 outcomes / 1 suggestion / 6-person roster + economics (break-even $78.02, shop rate $85).

**⚠️ REMAINING:** (1) ✅ **Andrew eyeballed /reports — and it found a real rendering bug, now fixed and confirmed** (the margin bars sat on different-width tracks; see "Left off"). ⚠️ **The rest of that pass is still owed: diagnostic drawer opens on click, Margins card shows the Meridian alert, /suggestions shows the paneling row.** ⛔ **Before screenshotting, decide the `Math.abs(marginPct)` question — Meridian's −15% loss currently draws a 42.5%-wide bar, and that bar is in the shot;** (2) Cowork captures the screenshots (list + one expanded drawer); (3) ⛔ **the marketing site must label this data as illustrative/seeded, never as real client results** (brief requirement); swap in real beta-shop jobs when they exist.

**How:** `scripts/seed-demo-completed-projects.mjs` — 7 closed Tampa jobs, margins deliberately not all green, computed in-script from hours×$85+materials so the list, drawer waterfall and outcome row cannot disagree. Fixed UUIDs (`ba9d0000-…`), upserts (safe to re-run; re-running also repairs drift), preview-by-default, refuses Built's org AND any org holding projects it didn't create. Also `scripts/inspect-demo-org-state.mjs` (read-only). **Wiring verified against the code, not assumed:**
- The completed list + DiagnosticDrawer read **`project_outcomes` only**; nothing joins back to time entries at render.
- **Margin alerts only scan ACTIVE stages** (`sold`/`production`/`installed`) — a `complete` project can never alert. So job 7 (Meridian Storefront, the loss) sits at **`installed`**, the one stage that is both alert-eligible AND "closed" per `lib/closed-jobs.ts` (all draws received, no open clock-ins) → it appears in Completed Projects *and* fires the alert ($88.75/h effRate vs $78.02 derived break-even, threshold ×1.15 = $89.72). Break-even needs org economics, so the script seeds `orgs.overhead_inputs`/`team_members`/`billable_hours_inputs` + `team_compensation` (comp must NOT go in the jsonb roster — 087).
- ⛔ **`unit_price_override` on an estimate line ZEROES its hours** (`computeLineBuildup` short-circuit) — no seeded line uses it; hours ride `dept_hour_overrides` (qty 1) or the rate-book item × qty.
- The big_up suggestion (Finish/Install, "paint-grade wall paneling" item, ratio 1.25 across jobs 2+7) is seeded as a row AND its underlying evidence is real — each paneling sub holds exactly ONE book-linked line so `lib/closed-jobs.ts` attributes the sub's time entries to it cleanly. ⚠️ **Don't hit Re-scan on /suggestions before screenshots** — it keeps the suggestion active but rewrites the proposed hours in per-job-total units (classify() quirk); re-running the seed restores it.
- ⚠️ PostgREST can't `LIKE` a uuid column — both scripts filter ids in JS (the SQL seeds cast `id::text` for the same reason).

**Cleanup when real beta jobs replace this:** `node scripts/reset-org-data.mjs 36f655a7-b989-4073-bb9d-3c5585623738 --apply --i-understand` clears the work; it does NOT cover `item_suggestions` or the seeded `rate_book_items` row — the two `DELETE … WHERE id::text LIKE 'ba9d0000%'` statements are in the seed script header. Or just retire the org.

### Change order → draw schedule — ✅ **BUILT 2026-09-13 (`596b201`); migration `106` ✅ ON PROD AND VERIFIED** (`change_order_id` in the schema cache). tsc clean, build clean, `verify-co-draw` 15 checks, payment-ledger suite still passes.

**The problem, in one line: approving a change order grows `projects.bid_total` but never touches `cash_flow_receivables`, so the payments board silently inflates the FINAL draw by the CO amount with no label saying why.**
- Traced: `approveCo` (lib/change-orders:486) flips state → `applyApprovedCo` → `recomputeProjectBidTotal` → `appendCoToRollingInvoice`. **Nothing writes a draw row.**
- `reconcileProject` (lib/payment-ledger:168) then finds `remaining > unpaidSum` and does `last.scheduled += delta` — *"The contract grew (a change order) — the FINAL draw absorbs it whole."* The aggregate is right; the presentation is a lie by omission.
- **Three consequences:** the stored rows no longer sum to the contract (`drift` is computed and, as far as I can tell, never surfaced) · the client-facing final draw grows with no line item explaining it · **a CO approved after the final draw is paid silently re-opens a settled draw** (`last.scheduled` grows regardless of `covered`).

**⛔ ANDREW'S CALL, 2026-09-13: AN APPROVED PRICED CO GETS ITS OWN DRAW ROW. A CREDIT CO KEEPS SHRINKING THE FINAL DRAW (no negative rows on a client-facing schedule).**

**What shipped:** an approved PRICED CO appends one labelled draw (`CO-03 — {title}`) carrying `change_order_id`. Credits write nothing — the existing shrink-from-the-end branch handles them without pushing a draw below what's been paid. `$0` COs go through `finalizeFreeCo` and never reach it.

**⛔ THREE THINGS THAT WOULD EACH HAVE BEEN A SILENT BUG:**
1. **The unique partial index IS the idempotency.** `approveCo` runs its steps as separate best-effort try/catch blocks and a card can be re-approved, so "write it twice" is a real path. `23505` is caught and treated as success.
2. **⛔ `saveMilestones` DELETES EVERY `status='projected'` ROW ON THE PROJECT — which is exactly what a CO draw is.** Opening the milestone builder and saving would have destroyed the change order's line and put the board back to inflating the final draw, and the builder can't re-insert what it doesn't know about. Now guarded with `.is('change_order_id', null)` **plus a pre-106 fallback**, because PostgREST fails the whole statement on one unknown column and a builder that can't save would be the worse bug.
3. **Where the row sorts.** `reconcileAll` waterfalls received money over the draws in sort order, and `sort_order` has NO COLUMN — it's `order:N` inside `notes`. A CO row at the front soaks up payments belonging to earlier draws. The decision is `coDrawSlot` in `lib/payment-schedule` (pure, 15 checks) — it reads the MARKER not the array position, and **an undated final draw stays undated** rather than being given an invented month.

**⛔ DELIBERATE DEPARTURE FROM THE SCOPE ABOVE: a project with NO draws gets NO CO row.** The scope said "fall back to a month out" and that was wrong — a sold project with zero draws is listed in the "No payment schedule" tray, which is driven by *has no rows*. Writing one CO draw would pull the project OUT of the tray while the rest of its contract stayed unscheduled: **hiding an untracked job in order to show one line.** The tray already reports it at full `bid_total`, which includes the CO.

**⚠️ FORWARD-ONLY. NOT BACKFILLED.** COs approved before `106` still have stale rows and a quietly inflated final draw — see the next item.

### Surface `drift` — ✅ **BUILT 2026-09-17. No migration.** tsc clean, build clean, `verify-drift` 23 checks (all break-tested), `verify-payments-board` still 55, `verify-co-draw` still 15.

⛔ **THE SCOPE WAS HALF STALE: the per-project line ALREADY EXISTED** — added by `ee1c015` in the payments block two days after this item was written, and STATE never recorded it. *"Verified by grep"* was true on 2026-09-13 and false by the time anyone read it. **Check the code before building to a scoped note; a spec is a snapshot.**

**What was actually missing, and is now built:**
- **`driftByProject` on `OrgReconciliation`** — a sibling, as scoped; `reconcileAll`'s signature is untouched. Plus `driftsWorthShowing()` and `DRIFT_EPS = 1`, and `storedSum` is now exposed on `ProjectReconciliation` so a UI prints the real figure instead of reconstructing it as `contractTotal + drift`.
- **A tray on `/payments`** — "Schedule doesn't match the contract", each row linking to the project. ⚠️ **NO "fix it" button, deliberately** (the scope's own warning: auto-backfilling CO rows means guessing dates and labels, and this codebase has twice shipped a screen that silently rewrote money).

⛔ **TWO WAYS DRIFT LIES, BOTH FIXED, NEITHER VISIBLE TO tsc:**
1. **NO CONTRACT VALUE IS NOT "ZERO DRIFT" AND NOT "HUGE DRIFT".** `loadOrgPayments` builds totals with `Number(bid_total) || 0`, so a project with no contract arrives as **0, not undefined** — the `??` fallback never fires and `storedSum − 0` reports the ENTIRE SCHEDULE as drift. **The existing per-project line would have told Andrew "UT - Public Arts is $27,425 over the contract" when nobody ever recorded a contract** — blaming the schedule for a missing project total. Now `contractKnown` carries it and both surfaces print a different sentence. It sorts FIRST in the tray.
2. **PROJECTS WITH NO DRAWS ARE EXCLUDED ENTIRELY.** Their `storedSum` is 0, so the arithmetic reports each whole contract as drift — every un-scheduled job would appear in BOTH trays, the second time as a huge negative (**Leonard renders as −$80,000**). The "No payment schedule" tray already owns those ~$600k.

✅ **MEASURED ON PROD BEFORE BUILDING, because the field's own doc says "NON-ZERO IS THE NORMAL STATE TODAY" — that is no longer true and a tray would have been wallpaper if it were.** 4 of 17 scheduled projects drift (24%), none under $100 (the $1 rounding era is genuinely repaired). **In BUILT, the tray shows exactly two rows: UT - Public Arts (no contract value, $27,425 of draws) and Towers (+$2,474)** — precisely the two STATE already flagged. ⚠️ **Batia correctly does NOT appear** (its gap is MillSuite vs Built's number, not draws vs `bid_total`). ⚠️ **The Bayside demo org shows THREE drifting jobs** — the seed wrote receivables against `actual_revenue` while `bid_total` differs; harmless, but it will be in shot if /payments is ever screenshotted for marketing. `scripts/inspect-drift.mjs` runs the SHIPPED lib against the real book so the tray and the report cannot be two different answers.

### Drawing parser: use the PDF text layer — scoped 2026-09-13. **No migration. ⚠️ Touches the estimate takeoff, which every new job runs through.**

**`/api/parse-drawings` asks a vision model to guess at dimensions that are sitting in the file as extractable text.**
- `extractTextFromPdf` (lib/pdf-parser:215) pulls the whole text layer, and the only thing done with it is `probe.text.trim().length < TEXT_EMPTY_THRESHOLD` — a 100-char check to guess "is this scanned?". **It is then discarded so completely that the API path sets `text: ''` on the envelope** (lines 743/804/836).
- **Proven on `Approval_Murtagh_Bar.pdf`:** the text layer contains `19-1/8"` ×4 (the door count), `EQ` ×4 (the bar panels), the full shelf-spacing string `16-3/4 / 3/8 / 13-5/8 / 3/8 / 13-5/8 / 3/8 / 15-7/8`, every callout, and the title block. All plain text. This is why the BOM parser guessed 8 drawers where there were 4 — **and the ESTIMATE takeoff is working from the same blindfold.**

**Build — two steps, and STOP AFTER STEP 1 IF IT'S ENOUGH:**
1. **Send the text.** Thread `probe.text` through `parseViaApi` → the route → a second `{type:'text'}` content block alongside the document block, under a heading like `EXTRACTED TEXT LAYER (verbatim, authoritative for dimensions)`. Add one prompt paragraph: **prefer a dimension string over a visual estimate; a dimension repeated N times is a count of N.** Stop the envelope lying about `text: ''` while you're in there.
2. **Only if step 1 under-delivers: positions.** pdfjs gives `transform` per item, so strings can be clustered by x/y into views ("FRONT ELEVATION 2/2"). Then: *the same cabinet appears in several views — take the maximum a single view supports, never the sum.* That's the structural fix for cross-view double counting. **Materially more work; don't pre-build it.**

**⚠️ Traps:**
- **`extractTextFromPdf` caps at 8 pages and 20k chars.** Fine for Murtagh (2 pages); a 30-sheet commercial set gets a partial text layer, and partial is worse than none if the prompt calls it authoritative. **Decide the cap deliberately and say in the prompt that it may be truncated.**
- **A scanned set has no text layer** — the existing `isScanned` path must keep working untouched.
- ⛔ **THIS IS THE SALES INTAKE PATH.** A prompt change that improves counts but degrades room grouping or LF is a net loss. There is no regression harness.

**Verify — and this is the part that needs Andrew:** re-parse 2–3 real sets that already produced good estimates (Kennedy, Forsythe, Murtagh) and **compare before/after side by side**. Rooms, LF and item grouping must not get worse; dimensions should get better. **If it's not clearly better on a real set, revert it** — the BOM parser is the cautionary tale: plausible reasoning, no verification against reality, a day burned.

### Supplies directory ✅ · BOM parser ⛔ KILLED — scoped 2026-09-12, one survived.

**1. ✅ SUPPLIES DIRECTORY — LIVE.** `/supplies` under Manage (`2cc1f46`); migration `102` ✅ ON PROD AND VERIFIED. **Andrew has already added a supply.** Name (linked when a URL is set), vendor, freeform phone/rep/account, notes; inline add/edit, **archive rather than delete**, one search box.
- **No categories, no filters — Andrew's call.** ⚠️ The search therefore covers EVERY field and ANDs terms across the whole row: "klingspor sandpaper" finds the row whose *vendor* is one word and *name* the other. Where filters would go is marked in the page header.
- **⛔ NOT A PRICED CATALOG AND MUST NOT BECOME ONE** — the rate book owns anything that prices a job. Written into migration 102.
- **⛔ `url` IS AN XSS SURFACE** — through the `lib/task-links` allowlist on the way OUT of the database as well as in, because a row can reach Postgres without passing through the app.
- The pure half is `lib/supply-item.ts` so the verify script needs no Supabase client — the `verify-payments` lesson applied up front. 25 checks.

**2. ⛔ BOM PARSER — BUILT 2026-09-12 (`6c2a79a`), KILLED 2026-09-13 (`4c8331c`); migration `105` DROPS THE TABLE.** Andrew, after one test against a real approval set: *"the only thing that matters is the sheet count. we can read '4 drawers' more quickly than parsing. we dont need AI to tell us we need to order 8 pieces of glass."*

**⛔ DO NOT REBUILD THIS WITHOUT READING THE NEXT TWO PARAGRAPHS.**

- **What happened.** Tested on `Approval_Murtagh_Bar.pdf`. Every count wrong, most by 2×: **drawers 4 → 8** · mesh inserts 4 → 6 (plus a duplicate line for the same material) · doors 8 → 12 · glass shelves 8 → 6 · **sheet goods: unknowable → "30", stated confidently.** The double-count was structural — the same cabinet appears in FIVE views across two sheets, and the model's own note admitted it: *"section and front elevation suggest additional drawers."*
- **⛔ THE COUNTS WERE PROBABLY FIXABLE. THAT WASN'T THE PROBLEM.** The PDF carries a full text layer with `19-1/8" ×4` and `EQ ×4` literally in it — feeding that through would likely have fixed the counting. **It still wouldn't have been worth having.** The only number the shop can't get FASTER BY LOOKING is the **sheet count**, and that needs part sizes + nesting, which happens on the **PRODUCTION** drawings, not the approval set. Everything the parser could ever be good at is something a human reads off an elevation in seconds. **Automating the easy half of a job while being unable to touch the hard half is not worth its maintenance.**
- **⚠️ IF SOMEONE REVIVES THIS, THE FIRST QUESTION IS "CAN IT PRODUCE A SHEET COUNT" — NOT "CAN IT COUNT ACCURATELY".** If the answer is no, stop. Accurate counts were never the blocker.
- **Also learned and discarded with it:** the estimate can't supply counts either — Andrew: *"we'll add more of something to inflate the price and hopefully make up for the custom thing that we don't have a composer for."* **The estimate is a PRICING artifact, not a physical bill of materials.** Anything that tries to derive real quantities from `estimate_lines` runs into this.
- Removed cleanly: `BomSection`, `lib/bom`, `lib/bom-merge`, `verify-bom-merge`, the `mode` branch + BOM prompts in `/api/parse-drawings`, `parsePdfForBom`, the project-page mount. **The estimate takeoff path is byte-for-byte what it was before the branch existed.** 1,389 lines out.

**⛔ TWO FINDINGS FROM THE EXERCISE THAT OUTLIVED THE FEATURE — these are worth more than the BOM was:**
1. **`/api/parse-drawings` IGNORES THE PDF TEXT LAYER ENTIRELY.** `lib/pdf-parser` extracts the full text and uses it ONLY to measure its LENGTH (a `<100 chars ⇒ scanned?` check), then throws it away. **The ESTIMATE takeoff — which Built actually uses on every new job — is asking a vision model to guess at dimensions that are sitting in the file as extractable text.** Verified on Murtagh: `19-1/8"` ×4, `EQ` ×4, the full shelf-spacing string, every callout, all of it plain text. **This is a live, cheap improvement to a feature that matters. Not yet scoped.**
2. **The composer prices doors by LINEAR FOOT, not by count** (`qty_doors` is LF). There is no door count anywhere in the estimate. `drawerCount`, `endPanels` and `fillers` ARE true counts. Worth knowing before anything else tries to read quantities out of a job.

### Home consolidation + sales goal — ✅ **ALL THREE BUILT 2026-09-12** (`3a90266` item 1 · `5b0f2b3` item 2 · `76cfda4` item 3); **migration `101` ✅ ON PROD AND VERIFIED. Nothing blocking — only Andrew's setup pass + one open question.** tsc clean, production build clean, tour targets PASS 57/44, three verification scripts pass (44 + 30 + 28 checks).

**Andrew's pass:** (1) **Settings → Sales goal** — set material % + profit %; ⚠️ **pin the monthly fixed cost if Kaylin should see the goal**, because the derived figure needs payroll and payroll is owner-only in the database. (2) `/pm` is now the home — check the logo, the Watch list and Invoiced cards. (3) `/reports` for the AI report.

**1. ✅ AI shop report → `/reports`** (`3a90266`). Now `app/(app)/reports/components/AiShopReport.tsx`, sitting between "what happened" and "what's coming".
- **⛔ AND IT HAD NO AUTHENTICATION AT ALL — FIXED IN THE SAME COMMIT.** `/api/shop-report` read `org_id` **from the request body** and handed that org's projects, time entries, invoices, shop-rate settings and name to Claude using the **service role**, which bypasses RLS — so migration 100 did nothing for it. Anyone able to obtain or guess an org UUID could POST it and get back a written narrative of another shop's pipeline, bid values, margins and hours. **Worse than a raw table read, because the answer arrives pre-summarised.** The org now comes from the Bearer token (`resolveApiCaller`); the body's `org_id` is **ignored outright** rather than validated, so there's no mismatch error for someone to "fix" later.

**2. ✅ `/dashboard` RETIRED; `/pm` IS THE HOME** (`5b0f2b3`). Logo, login, signup, checkout success, password reset, shop login, RoleGate and the PWA manifest all land on `/pm`. `/dashboard` stays as a **redirect** — bookmarks, already-installed PWAs and pre-deploy Stripe links still point at it.
- **Moved:** setup checklist · welcome toast · Projects at risk (now **"Watch list"**) · Receivables (now **"Invoiced"**). **Dropped, Andrew's call:** the key-metrics strip and the quick-actions tiles (all three tiles were already in the nav).
- **⛔ RECEIVABLES WAS WRONG, NOT JUST RELOCATED — three bugs, now `lib/receivables.ts` (pure) + 30 checks.**
  1. **It read `client_invoices.amount_received` alone, which a payment recorded on `/payments` never writes.** QB mode returns before touching the invoice; the only writer is the /qb-reconciliation path. **Andrew's $13,114 was invisible — that invoice read as fully outstanding and went overdue on its due date.**
  2. **A null `due_date` vanished** — an if/else-if chain with no final `else`, and every comparison against null is false. **Third instance of that failure class** (Leonard, undated draws on /pm, now this).
  3. **"Today" was the UTC day**, so invoices due today read as overdue from 8pm Eastern.
- **⛔ TWO MORE FOUND IN REVIEW, BOTH THE INVERSE SHAPE:** loading only OPEN invoices let a settled invoice's ledger cash spill onto the open ones and **erase real balances**; and pouring the whole `max()` re-allocated receipts that were already allocated, **mis-bucketing overdue vs due-later while the total stayed right** — which is exactly what hid it. Rule now: each invoice keeps its own receipts, only the ledger SURPLUS waterfalls.
- **⛔ `RESERVED_SLUGS` WAS MISSING `payments`, `pm` AND `tasks`** — every route added in the preceding week. `auth-context` treats an unreserved first segment as a **public** shop-login path, so none of them redirected a logged-out visitor to /login (RLS still refused the data, so this was a broken door rather than an open one), and a shop could have been handed the slug "pm". **Making /pm the home turned that from untidy into load-bearing.** `scripts/verify-reserved-slugs.mjs` now reads the routes off disk so the list can't rot again — **⚠️ NEW APP ROUTE ⇒ IT WILL FAIL UNTIL YOU ADD THE SLUG.** Confirmed against prod that no org holds any of the three.
- **Also from review:** the welcome toast **never fired for the person it exists for** (the overlay finishes on top of an already-mounted /pm and pushes to /pm — same route segment, so a `[]`-deps effect never re-runs; now announced + listened for, `lib/welcome-toast`) · the **first-project CTA** was nearly dropped, which would have left a fresh owner on a home page of "Nothing" with nothing saying *make a job* · **"your login isn't linked" greeted every brand-new owner**, whose roster is empty by definition.

**3. ✅ SALES GOAL BUILT; MIGRATION `101` ✅ ON PROD AND VERIFIED** (`76cfda4`; verified 2026-09-12 — all three `orgs` columns answer 200 through PostgREST, so they're in the schema cache). `goal = monthlyFixed ÷ (1 − materialPct − profitPct)`; fixed cost derives from the **same numerator as the shop rate** (all overhead + all payroll), overridable; material % settable with a learned suggestion; profit % assumed. The draw sum survives as **"Scheduled draws"**. `/payments` gets the banner, `/pm` mirrors it, Settings gets the knobs.
- **All three columns nullable, no DEFAULT, deliberately** — a default would have manufactured a confident wrong revenue target for every org the day it ran.
- **⛔ AN ADMIN AND THE OWNER SAW DIFFERENT GOALS — 4× apart on a test org.** Team comp is owner-only at the DB level (087) and `loadTeamComp` returns an **empty map rather than an error**, so a manager's derived fixed cost is overhead alone. Now reports `blind` and says why; **pinning the override is what makes the goal shareable.**
- **Other review catches:** `/payments` rendered "$0 received · 0% of target" pre-099, contradicting its own header and disagreeing with /pm · `loadShopRateSetup` **throws** and three new callers didn't catch it (the settings card sat on "Loading…" forever with no error and no Save) · the material suggestion **sampled in-flight jobs**, which have all of their price and only some of their bills — understating material by ~2/3 in a worked example, made worse by the price weighting (**finished jobs only** now) · it could suggest **>100%** and offer a "Use it" button that put the goal straight into its error state · `goalProgress` guarded the divisor but not the numerator, and a **NaN width is invalid CSS so the bar fell back to FULL**.
- **✅ MODEL GAP RESOLVED AND BUILT 2026-09-12 (`c70f8d3`) — consumables DERIVE from material × markup.** Consumables were a fourth destination the three-way split ignored (a 7%-of-revenue consumable spend moves an $80,000 goal to $93,023). The divisor is now **`1 − materialPct − (materialPct × org consumable markup) − profitPct`** — **no fourth setting**, because consumables already ride on material everywhere else (`consumablesCost = materialCost × consumableMarkupPct`). `materialPct` stays **MATERIAL ONLY**; the settings card shows the derived consumables % so the arithmetic is legible.
  - **✅ Both acceptance criteria verified** in `verify-sales-goal` (57 checks): the worked example reproduces to the cent ($40k fixed, 30% material, 20% profit, 23.33% markup → **$93,023.26**), and **an org with 0 markup sees an unchanged goal**.
  - **⛔ THE CAP COUNTS THE DERIVED POINTS TOO.** Checking only material + profit let three individually reasonable inputs drive the divisor to zero through the back door — 60% material + 20% profit is under the cap until a 40% markup adds 24 more. Pinned with a teeth-check.
  - **⚠️ The markup defaults to 0, NOT to pricing's 10.** A caller that forgets to pass it gets the old, smaller goal rather than a silently inflated target someone plans against.
  - **⚠️ Double-count warning is live:** the card names any overhead category that looks like consumables (**'Shop consumables'** and **'Tools'** ship as defaults) and says it's now counted twice — it does **not** silently exclude it, because which side is right is the shop's call. ⛔ **Andrew: check Settings → overhead for those two lines.**
  - _Decision note: asked in-session, Andrew picked "fold into material %"; the Cowork pass had already scoped the derived version. Per CLAUDE.md the current STATE is truth, and both agree on what matters — the shop still sets exactly one number._

### Small fixes wave 4 — ✅ ALL EIGHT BUILT 2026-09-11. **Only blocker: migration `098` on prod (see CURRENT FOCUS). Then Andrew's live pass.**

**✅ MIGRATION `098` RAN ON PROD 2026-09-11 AND IS VERIFIED** — `tasks.links`, `tasks.tags`, `orgs.task_tags` all PASS and are in the schema cache. Links and tags are live.

**⚠️ THE PRE-098 FALLBACK IS STILL IN `lib/tasks` AND MUST STAY.** It is now dormant, not dead: PostgREST fails an ENTIRE select on one unknown column, so the module asks for these columns optimistically and, on 42703/PGRST204, drops them for the session and retries. That is what let this deploy ship before the migration, and it is the same net any FUTURE task column will need. It also covers a real case that isn't hypothetical — **a customer org restored from an older schema, or a 098 that gets rolled back.** Don't "simplify" it away now that the columns exist. Re-probe any time with:
`node --env-file=.env.local scripts/verify-migration.mjs tasks:links,tags orgs:task_tags`

**1 ✅ Who added a task.** ⛔ **`created_by` IS A LOGIN ID (`users.id`); `assignee_ids` ARE ROSTER IDS (`orgs.team_members[].id`).** Two id spaces that look identical and fail SILENTLY when swapped. `users_select_self` (084) forbids the browser reading anyone else's `users` row, so the only client-side resolution is the roster's `user_id` bridge — that's `nameByUserId` in the provider, deliberately a **different map** from `nameById`. An unlinked login can't be named at all, so the line is omitted rather than printing "Unknown" on every task in a shop that hasn't done the /team linking pass.
- **This bug was ALREADY SHIPPED and nobody had noticed:** the comment header looked an `author_user_id` up in the ROSTER map, never matched, and attributed **every comment in the system** to "Someone". Fixed in the same commit.

**2 ✅ Archive (no new page).** Completed tasks are kept forever. ⚠️ **`listTasks` IS OPEN-ONLY NOW** — completed rows no longer ride along with the list, because fetching a shop's whole history on every page load to render a *collapsed* section is absurd. `listArchivedTasks` loads on expand. Respects the person AND tag filters (that's the feature: person + Archive = that person's archive), groups by month past 12 rows, **capped at 500 and it says so when capped**.

**3 ✅ Links.** ⛔ **AN HREF IS EXECUTABLE.** `javascript:` in a task link is stored XSS against every manager who opens that task. `lib/task-links` is an **allowlist** (http/https/mailto/tel), not a blocklist, and strips the control + zero-width characters a browser ignores *before* testing the scheme — otherwise `java\nscript:` runs. Comment URLs auto-link **display-only**; the stored body is never rewritten. 38 cases in `scripts/verify-task-links.mjs`.
- ⚠️ **One real bug came out of those tests:** the strip removed ` ` too, so the "this is prose, not a URL" guard ran *after* its own evidence was deleted and **"call Dave tomorrow" became `https://calldavetomorrow`**. Order is load-bearing; there's a comment saying so.

**4 ✅ Done timestamps.** One formatter (`formatDoneAt`) so the archive row and the detail can't drift. Year printed only when it isn't the current one — an archive kept forever eventually holds two Septembers. ⚠️ The copy in `TaskRow` is defensive, not live (a TaskRow's `done_at` is always null now); `TaskArchive` is what actually renders it.

**5 ✅ Tags.** Inline create from the row, manage popover for rename/recolour, filter row beside the people. `orgs` write goes through `updateOrgChecked`. ⛔ **Tasks store tag NAMES — the registry is only the picker and the colour**, so a rename does NOT rewrite tasks already tagged; they keep the old name and fall back to neutral. The rename warning in the UI is not decoration.

**6 ✅ Project chip → link.** ⛔ **`stopPropagation` COULD NOT HAVE FIXED THIS.** The collapsed row was ONE `<button>` wrapping the title *and* the chips, and an `<a>` inside a `<button>` is invalid HTML. The title is now the expand affordance and the chip row is its sibling — which also makes the chip a **real anchor**, so cmd-click and middle-click open the job in a new tab.

**7 ✅ Kanban search.** Filters across all columns; empty columns keep their headers; an all-empty board says "No projects match X". The predicate was **extracted to `lib/project-search` and the projects dashboard now reads it too** — a second inline copy is how "it finds it on one page but not the other" starts. No debounce (in-memory array; a delay would only feel like lag).

**8 ✅ Subproject PRICE on the project page.** Cards priced their own buckets through `computeBucketedPrice`; cost kept as the quiet second figure, omitted when the two are equal.
- ⛔ **THE MARGINS OBJECT IS THE PROJECT'S, NOT A FRESH ONE.** That is the whole reason an IMPORTED job stays frozen — the importer pins margins to 0, so price == cost. Re-deriving margins here would have been the **FOURTH** surface to re-price a frozen job.
- **Why the sum works:** `computeBucketedPrice` is **linear per bucket** (`priceFromMargin` is `cost/(1-f)`), so exact per-sub prices already sum to the project price. Only whole-dollar *display* rounding breaks it. `lib/allocate` fixes exactly that with largest-remainder. **That linearity is ASSERTED, not assumed** — add a minimum, a cap or a tiered rate and `scripts/verify-subproject-price.mjs` fails loudly instead of the cards quietly drifting.
- ⛔ **DISPLAY ONLY — never written back.** And when the parts genuinely don't sum to the whole (>$1 drift = something in the total belongs to no subproject) it **refuses to allocate and warns**, because forcing the sum would smear that amount invisibly across the cards. The subproject PAGE stays cost-only.
- ⚠️ **Known asymmetry, pre-existing:** the QuickBooks seed lines still round each sub independently, so QB line prices can miss the project total by a dollar where the cards now can't.

**⚠️ FOUR REAL BUGS CAME OUT OF THE REVIEW PASS, all fixed before commit — worth knowing because three were in the pre-098 fallback, which is the code path running RIGHT NOW:**
- the "098 present?" flag flipped back to `true` on the BASE retry, so it **oscillated every refresh** — tags and links appeared and vanished on alternate saves;
- gating the retry on the SHARED flag meant two concurrent queries raced and the loser **skipped its retry and returned an EMPTY TASK LIST** (reachable: the Archive toggle is clickable during the first load);
- `createTask` had **no fallback at all** — its RETURNING clause names the new columns, so one stale flag left "New task" broken for the rest of the session;
- two copies of the tag-filter hook raced over one localStorage key, so the drawer and `/tasks` showed **different filters** (the drawer is mounted app-wide and returns null *after* its hooks run, so both were live at once). It lives in the provider now.
Also: a failed archive fetch no longer counts as loaded (it said "Nothing completed yet" and cached that forever), the archive resets on org change, and the "Manage tags" gear can now close its own popover.

### Upcoming payments dashboard (`/payments`) — ✅ BUILT 2026-09-11 (`ecf9d37` data · `5889810` milestone fixes · `f7371ce` the board). **NO MIGRATION. Nothing blocking — Andrew's live pass is all that's left.**

**⛔ TWO THINGS THE SCOPE NOTE GOT WRONG. Both were checked, not trusted:**
- **The table is `cash_flow_receivables` (`type='receivable'`), NOT `project_milestones`.** There is no project_milestones table; milestones piggyback on receivables (see `lib/milestones`).
- **NO MIGRATION WAS NEEDED.** The note asked whether marking received records WHEN and budgeted a migration if not. **It always has** — `markMilestoneReceived` stamps `received_date`, verified present on prod. It just wasn't on the `ProjectMilestone` type, which is why it looked absent. It is now.

**⛔ THE TIMEZONE TRAP — read before touching any date here.** `expected_date`/`received_date` are DATE columns (bare 'YYYY-MM-DD'). `new Date('2026-09-01')` is UTC midnight = **Aug 31 20:00 in New York**, so `.getMonth()` says August. **Every draw dated the 1st would have rendered in the previous month** — silently, and only for shops west of Greenwich, which is all of them. Confirmed by hand before the guard was written. Use `parseLocalDate`/`formatLocalDate`; never `new Date(str)`, never `.toISOString()` on a calendar day. `scripts/verify-payments.mjs` pins it and **is run in three timezones**.
- **`lib/payment-schedule` is PURE (no database import) on purpose** — the guard first lived in `lib/payments`, which builds a Supabase client at module scope, so it threw `supabaseUrl is required` before its first assertion. **A safety net that can't run isn't one.** It now needs no credentials and no network. Don't re-merge the two modules.

**TWO DATES, NOT INTERCHANGEABLE.** `expected_date` drives "needed this month"; `received_date` drives "what came in this month". A draw expected in August but paid in September is **September cash and August shortfall**.

**What's on the page:** rolling 3-month columns (◀ Today ▶), Needed + Received per month, and two trays ABOVE the board — **Past due** (relative to TODAY, never the window: deciding it from the window start painted this month's un-due draws red the moment you paged forward) and **No date set**. Received cards are inert (their date is a record, not a plan). Dragging writes `expected_date` and nothing else, and treats a zero-row update as failure.
- ⚠️ **Firefox needed `dataTransfer.setData` on dragstart** — without it the board is entirely undraggable there while working fine in Chrome/Safari. Worth knowing for the kanban and /schedule too.
- `scripts/verify-payments-query.mjs` asks the real database whether the embedded join + embedded stage filter resolve. **That's PostgREST syntax, not TypeScript** — `tsc` happily compiles a select string the server rejects.

**⛔ THREE REAL BUGS IN EXISTING MILESTONE CODE, found building this and fixed (`5889810`). /payments is the first surface that sums these rows into a headline number, so all three went from latent to visible:**
1. **Saving milestones DUPLICATED every received one.** `saveMilestones` deletes only `status='projected'` but re-inserted the caller's ENTIRE list as projected — and the project page passed every milestone, stripped of status. A received milestone survived the delete AND got a projected copy: the project scheduled **more than 100%** of its value and /payments counted that money **twice** (once received, once needed). Now only what the delete removed is re-inserted, `status` travels from the call site, and the **original index is carried through the filter** because `sort_order` is `order:N` in `notes` and the surviving rows keep the N they had.
2. **`received_date` was stamped with the UTC day** — `toISOString().slice(0,10)` rolls at 8pm Eastern, so an evening payment recorded as arriving *tomorrow*, and on a month's last evening in the *next month*. That's the exact date this page buckets by.
3. **The optimistic "received" update wrote `expected_date`** (a fabricated today) instead of `received_date`. Cosmetic alone; combined with (1) the next Save **persisted that invented forecast date**.

**⚠️ STILL TRUE, NOT FIXED — the project page and /payments can race.** `saveMilestones` still DELETES and re-inserts projected rows, so a drag on /payments is lost if a project page was **already mounted before the drag** and is saved after (its in-memory `expected_date` is stale, and the builder has no UI to show or edit that field). Fresh-load-then-save preserves it, which is the common path. **The real fix is to make `saveMilestones` update-in-place instead of delete+insert** — that also keeps row ids stable, so an open /payments tab doesn't hold dead ids. Not done; it's a change to a core write path and wanted its own pass.

**Andrew's live pass:** every unpaid draw appears exactly once · drag persists across reload · received moves from Needed to Received · **month totals foot by hand against 2–3 real projects** (the query is verified, the totals are not — that needs a signed-in session).

### Payments v2 — ledger + schedule. **✅ BUILT 2026-09-11 (`b0eee04`); MIGRATION `099` ✅ ON PROD AND VERIFIED.** Nothing blocking — Andrew's live pass is all that's left.

**✅ MIGRATION `099` RAN ON PROD 2026-09-11 AND IS VERIFIED** — all eleven columns of `project_payments` are in the schema cache. Logging payments is live.
- ⚠️ **The missing-table fallback in `loadOrgLedger` STAYS.** It's dormant, not dead: it's what let this deploy ship before the migration, and it's the net any future ledger column will need. Don't "simplify" it away.
- **RLS: `project_payments` is in `scripts/rls-audit.mjs` now, but reports "empty — no signal" until there's data.** ⚠️ **Re-run the audit after the first real payment** to confirm it's actually protected. (The policy is in the same `BEGIN…COMMIT` as the table, so the table existing proves it committed — but this table holds money and deserves the direct check.) The same pass confirmed wave 4's tables ARE protected: `tasks` 0 of 62 rows visible, `task_comments` 0 of 5.

**THE STRUCTURAL FIX.** A milestone row was BOTH the plan and the payment record (`status='received'` + the PROJECTED amount), so a client who paid a different number had nowhere to put it. Andrew, on Built OS: *"when someone pays a different amount than what we're projecting… getting the math to link was not working for a while."* Now:
```
received  = sum(ledger)
remaining = contract total − received
the UNPAID part of the schedule rebalances to equal `remaining`,
with the FINAL draw absorbing the difference.
```
Partial, overpaid and out-of-order payments are just rows. **⛔ "MARK RECEIVED" IS GONE — you log a payment and paid-ness is DERIVED** (`reconcileProject`).

**⛔ `client_invoice_payments` (041) COULD NOT BE REUSED** — `invoice_id NOT NULL`. You cannot log a payment in MillSuite without an invoice, and **Built's invoices live in QuickBooks**. That is almost certainly why this never worked. `099` is a PROJECT-level ledger with no invoice FK. Negative amounts allowed (refunds/corrections); zero refused.

**⛔ NOTHING IS WRITTEN BACK.** Draw amounts stay as authored; the balancing is derived on read. Two past bugs came from a screen silently persisting recomputed money (staleness banking a $0 material, handoff re-pricing a frozen import). **A ledger must never edit a contract.**

**⛔ CHANGE ORDERS WERE — AND OUTSIDE THIS PAGE STILL ARE — SILENTLY BROKEN.** Nothing updates draws when a CO lands (`lib/change-orders` never references `cash_flow_receivables`; grepped, zero hits), so **any job that's been re-priced has draws that no longer sum to its total.** The final draw now absorbs that automatically and `drift` reports it. ⚠️ **Worth checking a real re-priced job** — the stored rows are still stale even though the page now reads correctly.

**NO COMPLETION-TRIGGER DERIVATION — Andrew's call:** *"Just make each draw payment due a month apart as default. we will manually move them where appropriate as the schedule shifts."* The app can't know the jobsite wasn't ready; a wrong inference about when money is due is worse than none. `milestone_trigger` survives as a LABEL only. **Don't build due-detection.**

**WATERFALL, and its one accepted consequence:** money fills the oldest unpaid draw first. Paying the install draw early still shows the DEPOSIT as the thing that got paid. Fine for a cash-flow tool ("how much is in, how much is left"); note it before anyone reports it as a bug.

**Shape:** `lib/payment-schedule` (pure calendar — the timezone guard lives here and must stay credential-free) · `lib/payment-ledger` (pure: `reconcileProject`, `buildPaymentsView`) · `lib/payments` (data) · `/payments` (board + **Log a payment** modal) · `components/project/ProjectPaymentLedger` (post-sale, read-only).
- **Project page: pre-sale composes the schedule; post-sale the builder is REPLACED** by the read-only ledger + a link. Andrew: *"ledger only on project page."*
- **QuickBooks: manual entry, no auto-matching.** Bookkeeping stays in QB. `qb_event_id` is a hook, not a feature.

**Verified:** `scripts/verify-payment-ledger.mjs` — **500 fuzzed schedules** asserting the one invariant (`received + outstanding == contract total`, no draw ever negative) plus the named cases: odd amounts, overflow, out-of-order, CO up, CO down, a cut deep enough to walk backwards past a paid draw, overpayment-as-credit, refunds, cents. `scripts/verify-payments.mjs` still runs the calendar guard in three timezones.

**⛔ TWO BUGS ANDREW HIT ON THE FIRST REAL PAYMENT — FIXED (`634c0eb`).** Murtagh Bar, an imported job. Both were reproduced before fixing, and both regression tests were confirmed to FAIL against the old code.
1. **A PHANTOM "$1" CARD.** Contract **$26,227 — an ODD number**. Half is $13,113.50, but the deposit draw was stored **rounded to $13,114** at save time. The client paid the true half, leaving **$0.50** "outstanding" — which at a $0.005 tolerance read `partial` and rendered as a card saying **"$1"** with the badge "$13,114 of $13,114 in" (both numbers round to the same figure, so it looked self-contradicting). ⛔ **Draws are stored in WHOLE DOLLARS; contracts and real payments carry CENTS.** The comparison must match the resolution the money is DISPLAYED at, so **a draw owing under $1 is now SETTLED**. A genuine part-payment still reads partial — the tolerance is a dollar, not a licence to round away debt.
2. **DRAGGING A CARD CHANGED WHICH DRAW WAS PAID.** `reconcileAll` sorted by `expectedDate` — *the exact field dragging writes* — so moving a card reordered the waterfall and the "paid" one jumped. Andrew: *"then changes when I move the new card."* ⛔ **Deposit-then-final is a property of the AGREEMENT; a forecast date is a guess about timing and must never decide which draw a payment settled.** Now ordered by the authored `sortOrder` (`order:N` in `notes`, as `rowToMilestone` reads it), created_at as tiebreak, and a row with no order sorts **last** so it can't hijack the deposit slot.
- **`reconcileAll` MOVED to `lib/payment-ledger`** — it's pure, and the sort it owns is what broke. ⚠️ Testing `reconcileProject` alone passes trivially (it takes the array already ordered), so a regression test **must** go through `reconcileAll`.
- ⚠️ **Rounded draws vs an odd contract is NORMAL, not rare.** Any contract that doesn't divide evenly by the percentages leaves sub-dollar residue. Expect it; don't "fix" it by rounding payments.

**✅ THE DEPOSIT GATE NOW READS THE LEDGER (`77ab790`).** Andrew: *"lets see how to link the downpayment being recorded and the production gateway."* Bonzer had $51,630 recorded and still said "Override: deposit forthcoming".
- **⛔ THE GATE WAS CHECKING SOMETHING A QB ORG CAN NEVER SATISFY.** `isDepositReceived` read `client_invoices.amount_received`, and **in QB mode nothing in this app writes that** (`markMilestoneReceived` deliberately returns early; money is meant to come from the watcher). **Built is a QB org, so the gate could not be satisfied on ANY job** — overriding was mandatory every time. That's why the override reads as the default: before the ledger it was the ONLY path.
- **Order is now:** `deposit_override` → **ledger net > 0** → contract invoice `amount_received > 0`. The ledger sits second because it's the most direct signal and needs no invoice to exist. **Net**, so a refund that cancels the deposit closes the gate again.
- **"Any net money" deliberately matches the invoice rule beside it**, rather than demanding the first draw be fully covered — a part-paid deposit the shop is happy to start on shouldn't need an override. ⚠️ Don't tighten this without asking; the override exists for the genuinely-forthcoming case.
- Degrades cleanly pre-099 (`projectReceivedTotal` → 0 → falls through). Incidentally **faster** for the common case: a paid project returns after two queries and skips the contract-invoice lookup.
- **Fixes all three consumers at once:** the projects-dashboard "Ready" badge, the project page, and pre-production.
- UI: **"Log the deposit"** now sits beside the override, linking to `/payments` — recording what actually happened must be at least as findable as skipping the check.

**⛔ SOLD JOBS WITH NO DRAW SCHEDULE WERE INVISIBLE — FIXED (`a825fcc`).** Andrew: *"i dont see any of the Leonard payments."* **Not the import** (Bonzer and Murtagh are imported and show fine) — Leonard, Kinser and Gus Bus simply have **no payment milestones**.
- **⛔ THE BOARD COULD NOT HAVE TOLD HIM.** `loadOrgPayments` selects FROM `cash_flow_receivables`, so **a project with zero draw rows produces zero rows** — the page cannot distinguish "no schedule" from "doesn't exist". **Three Leonard jobs worth $426,032 were invisible**, and the footer confidently read "$143,286 still owed across every sold job" while omitting them. **A cash-flow tool that silently drops contracts is worse than one that shows nothing.**
- Now: a **"No payment schedule" tray** lists every sold job with no draws (contract value + link), and the footer says how much contract value **isn't counted** instead of implying it has everything.
- **⛔ THIS ALSO EXPOSED A HOLE THE REDESIGN CREATED.** Locking the builder post-sale protects an AGREED schedule — but with no schedule there's nothing to protect, and hiding it left those jobs **permanently untrackable with no way to fix them**. The builder now shows on a sold job with **zero** milestones (with a note saying why) and reverts to the read-only ledger once a schedule exists. Gated on a loaded flag so it can't flash before the fetch lands.
- The Log-a-payment project picker was derived from the schedule too, so a job with no draws couldn't even be selected. It lists every sold job now.
- ⚠️ **GENERAL LESSON, worth applying elsewhere: any page built on an `!inner` join silently omits the parent rows that have no children.** Ask what's missing, not just what's shown.

**Andrew's live pass:** log a payment that DOESN'T match its draw and watch the final draw absorb it · a partial payment shows "X of Y in" · drag a draw between months · the project page shows ledger-only post-sale · foot a month's totals by hand.

**⚠️ Superseded:** the `saveMilestones` delete-and-re-insert race noted earlier is no longer worth its own pass for DATES (they're only edited on /payments now). The delete+insert still churns row ids on a pre-sale save, which is harmless today. Leave it.

### Manager dashboard (`/pm`) — ✅ BUILT 2026-09-11 (`fa14050`). **No migration. Nothing blocking — Andrew's live pass.**

**Andrew: "a project manager dashboard for Kaylin — we'll add more here later."** Built **generic**, not Kaylin-specific: every card is scoped to the signed-in viewer, so it's the same page for any manager. Nav: **"My day"** under Manage, ungated.
- **Today** — the viewer's own Today bucket, rendered with **the real `TaskRow`**, not a lookalike. Same Mine fallback as the nav badge (a login with no roster row sees everything, so the misconfiguration is visible rather than an undiagnosable empty list). "View all" → `/tasks`.
- **Money in** — this month's Needed / Received read from the **`/payments` data layer**, so the two can't disagree. Past due and No-date are their OWN labelled lines.
- **Quick upload** — `components/invoice-parser` reused as-is (no props; it reads `useAuth` internally).

**⛔ NO NEW ROLE CHECK, DELIBERATELY.** `RoleGate` already confines `member` to `/me`, so any route that isn't `/me` is owner/admin by construction; a second check is a second thing to keep in step. **The post-login landing is untouched.**
- ⚠️ **The scope note said "owner/admin/manager" — THERE IS NO `manager` ROLE.** The app has **owner / admin / member**. Kaylin is an admin.

**⛔ FOUR THINGS CAME OUT OF REVIEWING IT, ALL FIXED BEFORE COMMIT. The first is the one to remember:**
1. **UNDATED DRAWS WERE BEING DROPPED — the same failure class as the Leonard bug fixed hours earlier, reproduced immediately in brand-new code.** `buildPaymentsView` PARTITIONS its result (month buckets / `overdue` / `unscheduled`); reading only `months[0]` silently loses real money owed. **⚠️ GENERAL RULE: when a function partitions rows, consuming one bucket is how rows disappear. Account for every bucket or say why not.**
2. **Past-due money was folded into a figure labelled "September"**, which would have made this card disagree with `/payments` for the same month — contradicting the card's own reason for existing. Separate lines now, and every preview row SAYS whether it's past due / this month / undated (a gray date gives a manager no way to tell late from upcoming).
3. **`led.missing` was ignored**, so Received rendered a confident **"$0" that was a lie rather than a zero**. Shows `—` + a note now.
4. **`addTag` hardcoded gray.** The tag registry is **org-wide**, so a tag created from a /pm row would have been gray *everywhere, forever*. Matches the other two surfaces now.

**⚠️ ANDREW'S LOGIN ISN'T LINKED TO HIS ROSTER ROW — that's DATA, not a bug, but the UI was hiding it (`209b347`).** He reported /pm "bringing in tasks for everyone, not just me."
- **`myAssigneeId` bridges login → `orgs.team_members`, and it's only written when someone links that person on /team.** Until then nothing can be assigned to you, so the documented fallback shows **everyone** — deliberately, because a permanent empty list under your own name is undiagnosable.
- **⛔ A SILENT FALLBACK IS ONLY DEFENSIBLE WHILE IT'S VISIBLE.** It wasn't, so a list of Kaylin's and Hunter's work sat under the heading "Andrew's day" looking like a bug. **/pm** now shows an "everyone" chip + a banner with a link to /team; **/tasks** had the same gap under its "Mine" chip and got the same note; the drawer already said "Everyone's list". All three agree now. **Behaviour is unchanged — it just stops pretending.**
- **⛔ AND THERE WAS NO WAY TO DO IT — BUILT 2026-09-11 (`72783ba`).** Andrew: *"i dont see how to link my login."* He was right. Every action on `/api/admin/users` **created** a login (`create_login` / `set_role` / `reset_password` / `unlink`); **nothing pointed a roster row at an account that already existed.** So the commonest case had no path: **the OWNER**, who signs up first and builds the roster afterwards.
  - `create_login` could NOT substitute — it mints a NEW auth user and its role argument is `admin|member`, so using it on the owner's own row would have produced **a second, weaker account** beside the real one.
  - Added `list_logins` + `link_login`, and a **"Link existing"** button beside "Create login" on an unlinked row. The picker hides logins already claimed by another roster row (offering one twice would silently move it off the first person). `list_logins` runs service-role because it must: the browser can read only its OWN `users` row (`users_select_self`, 084).
  - ⛔ **`link_login` deliberately skips `checkAuthorityOver`, and that's safe:** linking grants NOTHING — it writes `team_members[].user_id`, which decides whose name a task shows under, not roles/passwords/access. Borrowing the privilege guard would block an admin from linking the OWNER, which is the main case.
- **⛔ …AND "Link existing" WASN'T ENOUGH EITHER — HIS ROW WAS ALREADY LINKED, TO THE WRONG ACCOUNT (`678e5eb`).** Andrew: *"i think its just my login that is effected."* Correct, and the screenshot proved it without a query: his row read **"Login active" + MANAGER**, and it offered **Reset password / Remove** + the ACCESS switch — all three of which render only when `role !== 'owner'`, while the switch itself renders only for an **owner** viewer. So the owner was looking at his own name bridged to a **second, admin-role account**. `myAssigneeId` stayed null; /pm sent him to /team; /team looked healthy; dead end.
  - **⛔ "Login active" IS NOT "THIS IS YOU", and nothing on the page said which.** Now: a **banner** when no roster row holds the signed-in login (asks the identical question `myAssigneeId` does — login id → roster row, never by name or email), and a **"You"** chip on the row that does hold it.
  - **⛔ RE-POINTING A ROW USED TO REQUIRE DELETING AN ACCOUNT.** The only exit from a linked row was "Remove" — and `unlink` does **not** unbridge, it calls `auth.admin.deleteUser` AND drops the `users` row, whose id is stamped on `tasks.created_by` and `task_comments.author_user_id`. **"Change login"** now re-points `team_members[].user_id` through the existing `link_login` path and touches nothing else.
  - ⚠️ **That button now reads "Delete login" and the confirm says the account is gone for good.** The old copy — *"will lose access to the worker app. Their roster entry and time entries stay"* — described a permissions change, which is a bad thing to be wrong about next to a red button.
- **✅ DONE 2026-09-12 — Andrew relinked and confirmed: "works now."** Mine is genuinely his on /pm, /tasks, the drawer and the nav badge; the "everyone" chip and banner are gone.
  - **The stray admin "Andrew" login is still there** — it costs a seat and nothing else. Delete it only if he wants the seat back. ⚠️ Its id may be stamped on `tasks.created_by` / `task_comments.author_user_id`, and deleting drops the `users` row, so those would stop resolving to a name (the UI omits the line rather than printing "Unknown" — it degrades, it doesn't break).
  - **⚠️ STILL OWED on the same /team visit: the "Gets tasks" trimming pass** — untick everyone but the managers + Hunter (open since wave 4).

**Andrew's live pass:** the Today card should show only YOUR tasks and stay in step with `/tasks` (check a task off in one, reload the other) · Needed/Received should match `/payments` for this month · drop an invoice on Quick upload.

**Room to grow** — Andrew's "we'll add more here later". Three cards in a 2/1 grid; add to the right-hand column.

### Subproject quantity ("QTY 4 of this cabinet") — ✅ BUILT 2026-09-04 (`39b4d67`); migration `097` ✅ ON PROD + VERIFIED. **Nothing blocking.**

`subprojects.quantity` — integer, NOT NULL, DEFAULT 1, CHECK ≥ 1. **The default is the safety property: every existing subproject keeps its exact price and the migration cannot move money on its own.** `scripts/verify-subproject-quantity.mjs` asserts that rather than assuming it (run with `npx tsx --env-file=.env.local`).
- **⛔ SCALING HAPPENS INSIDE `computeSubprojectRollup`, NOT AT THE CALL SITES.** Eight modules consume that rollup; scaling outside it would be eight chances to forget — which is exactly how one job ended up with three different prices on 09-04. Every site holding a subproject passes the quantity: project page, handoff, subproject page, `project-totals` (bid_total) and `project-hours` (schedule + capacity).
- **⛔ INSTALL IS DELIBERATELY NOT SCALED** (Andrew's call). It's an explicit site estimate — guys × days — and four cabinets in one room isn't four mobilisations. This falls out for free because the install prefill **isn't in this rollup**; consumers fold it in separately. **Don't "fix" that by scaling install.**
- **The subproject page reads `quantity` in its OWN select.** The main select lists explicit columns and renders the whole page — folding it in would 42703 the entire page pre-097.
- Verified: 15 cases — quantity 1 provably a no-op, linear cost/hours scaling, margin % unmoved, and 0 / negative / NaN / fractional inputs all refusing to zero, negate or fractionally price a subproject.
- ⚠️ **The `CHECK (quantity >= 1)` was NOT probed separately, on purpose** — the only test is writing a 0, and if the constraint were missing that write would zero a real subproject's price. It's in the same `BEGIN…COMMIT` as the column, so the column existing proves it committed.
- **✅ The estimate now prints the quantity (`5a2cb27`).** The Qty column was hard-coded to 1. ⛔ **The rate is DERIVED from the amount, never the reverse** — `amount` is authoritative because it's what keeps the estimate total equal to the project total.
  - **⛔ THE RATE CELL WITHHOLDS ITSELF RATHER THAN PRINT SOMETHING UNTRUE.** Qty × Rate must equal Amount *as printed*, on a document a client checks with a phone. Whole dollars break it ($11,871 ÷ 4 = $2,967.75; rounding to $2,968 prints "4 × $2,968 = $11,871"), and **some divisions can't reconcile at ANY precision** ($10,000 ÷ 3 = $3,333.33 → $9,999.99). When no true rate exists the cell prints `—` and the row reads "3 · — · $10,000". Verified across ten amount/qty pairs.
  - **⚠️ The QUICKBOOKS qty is deliberately untouched** — it still pushes qty 1 at the full amount. That drives a real invoice; Andrew asked about the estimate. **Decide before changing it.**
- Approvals/finish specs are one set per subproject regardless of quantity, which is right for a "(TYP)" unit.

### Kanban stage-chip colors — ✅ BUILT 2026-09-04 (`7331d4f`). **Nothing blocking; Andrew's live look is the only thing left.**

**There were THREE palettes and two already disagreed** — the part the spec didn't know. `StagePill` had sold **blue** / installed **green**; the projects dashboard had pre **amber** / installed **blue**; the kanban painted every stage the same green. The dashboard's palette is the truth, so **`STAGE_COLORS` now lives in `components/project/StagePill`** and all three read it: cover pill, dashboard badge, kanban chip. Note the cover pill therefore CHANGED — sold amber (was blue), installed blue (was green). Intended, not a regression.
- **`bidding` shares pre-production's amber.** Safe ONLY because `StagePill` renders one project at a time (project cover + pre-production header) — they can never sit side by side and the label distinguishes them. **If a stage pill ever appears in a LIST, give bidding its own colour first.**
- **A sold-and-READY job shows pre-production amber on the board but green on the dashboard.** The kanban has no approval data on a card so it can't tell them apart — a data gap, not a colour bug. Don't "fix" it by guessing readiness on the board.
- **Labels stay local to the dashboard** ("Pre-production" vs the pill's "Pre-Production", plus a Ready state the cover stages don't have). Only colour is shared.
- Verified by rendering both chips for all eight stages side by side — every row matched — and grep confirms no stage colour is hardcoded on the kanban page.

### Presentation estimate — round 3. **✅ ALL DONE + DEPLOYED 2026-09-04 (`c24dae1` modal · `8031967`+`d5a5e91` footer mark · `1fadef3`+`5bf1b06` cover redlines, two passes); migration `096` on prod; mark uploaded; **live pass done — Andrew verified real estimates on prod, round CLOSED** (only the HELD small-multi-package merge idea remains).**
