### Presentation estimate — Built's premium template. **✅ BUILT, DEPLOYED AND ITERATED ON REAL DATA (2026-09-03 → 09-04).** Visual spec was `mockups/estimate-presentation-mockup.html` (v3) — but see the letter-page note below: the mockup is a SCREEN document and its spacing does not survive contact with a page.

**✅ COMPLETE 2026-09-03 — migration `095` on prod, fonts vendored, both fixtures rendered and looked at.**
- **THE FONTS ARE COMMITTED AND MUST STAY.** The PDF route reads `assets/fonts/` from disk at request time, so a build without them renders every presentation estimate in Helvetica. Both families are OFL — redistribution is fine, the licence must travel with them, hence `assets/fonts/OFL-*.txt`. **Don't delete those.**
- **`google/fonts` SHIPS NEWSREADER AND INSTRUMENT SANS AS VARIABLE-ONLY.** Three automated routes failed on it: guessed static paths 404'd; the legacy-user-agent CSS trick now returns woff2; the variable-file fallback renders but **pins every weight to the default instance** — verified by rendering one string at 300/400/500/600 and getting four identical lines. **The ONLY source of the statics is the fonts.google.com download — each zip has a `static/` folder.** `lib/estimate-fonts.ts` scans for the `fvar` table and warns by name if a variable file reappears, because that failure is otherwise invisible: no error, the document just looks flat.
- **Newsreader is an optical-size family (9/14/24/36/60pt); we vendor the 14pt cut.** The serif runs 11.5-23pt here and 14pt covers that band — 9pt looks clunky at headline size, 24pt too fragile in the italic note. If the headline wants more contrast later, swap `Newsreader_24pt-Light` for that role only.

**⛔ RUN `npx next build` BEFORE DEPLOYING ANYTHING THAT TOUCHES A PDF COMPONENT — `tsc` IS NOT ENOUGH.** A deploy failed here (`8fe293d`) while git was perfectly clean, which is a confusing place to start debugging. Cause: sharing `estimateHeadlineFor` by exporting it from `EstimatePresentationPdf` made **`SendEstimateModal`, a CLIENT component**, import the PDF module — and with it `lib/estimate-fonts`, which reads `node:fs`/`node:path` to find the vendored TTFs. Webpack can't resolve those for the browser (`UnhandledSchemeError`). **`tsc --noEmit` compiles that arrangement happily: the server/client boundary is a BUNDLER concern, not a type one.** The helper now lives in `lib/estimate-headline.ts` with **zero imports, deliberately** — keep it that way, and never import a PDF component from client code.

**⛔ THE MOCKUP'S SPACING DOES NOT FIT A LETTER PAGE — know this before touching the layout.** The mockup is `min-height:1080px`; it GREW when content didn't fit and a page can't. Rendering it surfaced four bugs a type-check cannot see, all fixed:
- The **cover spilled onto a second sheet**, orphaning "Prepared for". Tightened density + **adaptive index row padding** (12 / 7 / 3 px past 11 and 16 subs) so it reads at 1 sub and at 20 — the 3px step was measured against Kennedy, where the total and "Prepared for" were being pushed onto a second sheet.
- **A long real title printed straight THROUGH its own price.** ⛔ **Neither `flexShrink` on the Text nor on a wrapping View fixed it** — react-pdf measures a Text at full content width and overlaps its sibling. Name/price is now a **fixed 68/32 split**, which cannot collide at any length; the cover index uses 78/22 for the same reason.
- A sub with **one** detail left a half-width dotted rule hanging in white space; a single detail now spans full width.
- The **signature block orphaned onto its own page**, and the footer printed only on the last page of a flow. ⛔ **Don't reintroduce a `flexGrow` spacer on the numbers page** — that's what pushed it over. The footer is `position:absolute` + `fixed`.

**⛔ WHAT REAL DATA BROKE (Kennedy EST-0017) — six things, none visible to a type-check or to my fixtures.**
- **The material never rendered.** Real descriptions NEST it: `Description - Material - Paint Grade Sheet Goods`. There is no section labelled "Material" to find, so it leaked into the detail grid still carrying its own prefix. Matched on the LINE now, wherever it sits.
- **"Details —" printed thirteen times down a page.** Everything after a `Details - …` line is a CONTINUATION of that section, and each line was being prefixed with the section label. Generic labels (`Details`, `Description`) are dropped; qualifying ones (`Dimensions`, `Hardware`, `Finish`) stay.
- **⛔ EVERY SCOPE BLOCK WAS `wrap={false}` → ONE SUBPROJECT PER PAGE.** A real block runs most of a page, so whenever two wouldn't fit the second moved whole, leaving ~40% of every sheet blank. Kennedy came out at **22 pages**. Now only the HEADER is held together (title + price + material) with `minPresenceAhead`, and the detail grid flows freely. **Don't put `wrap={false}` back on the section.**
- **The cover logo was wrong and is gone.** The mockup deliberately has none — the run header's wordmark is the branding — and an org logo above the kicker rendered as a stray centred mark while eating vertical budget the index needs.
- A very long detail **spans both columns** rather than stranding its neighbour beside a wall of text, and the column flow is walked so a final item that would sit alone widens instead of leaving a half-width rule.
- **⚠️ A 15pt LINE COST THREE PAGES.** Moving "Installation included" under the price added one line to every header; a taller `wrap={false}` header fails to fit more often and each miss wastes the rest of the page. 14 → 17 pages, recovered to 14 by tightening the block rhythm. **Measure page count after ANY change to the scope header** — `npx tsx scripts/preview-presentation-estimate.tsx` then count.

**Andrew's round-two notes, all applied (`e64ce64`, `63349b0`):** "From the shop" splits the org note on its own line breaks into spaced paragraphs (as one Text the newlines rendered at body line-height and a new sentence began flush against the previous one's wrapped tail) and promotes a trailing `- Andrew` line to the styled signature · "Installation included" moved under the price, smaller · **no "The work"** (it appeared twice and named the obvious) · client address on the cover beside the shop's · headline 32→27.

**⚠️ THE HEADLINE'S "the" IS CONDITIONAL, and a stale stamp can hide it.** `lib/estimate-headline.ts` adds the article only for households (`… Residence/Family/Estate`), never for a person — Built's client records hold both, and it's wrong for one of them either way. ⛔ **But three "Download PDF" buttons BYPASS the send modal** (`/estimates/[projectId]` and two on the project page). Those send no headline, so the route falls back to `projects.estimate_headline` — whatever was stamped by an earlier send. That's deliberate (a regenerate must reproduce what the client received) but it means **a wording change only takes effect via the send modal**, not the plain download button.

**⛔ ANSWERED 2026-09-04 — "can it be one long scrollable document, divided only when printing?" NO, not as a PDF.** Pagination is intrinsic to the format; there is no flow-now-divide-later. A single enormous page prints scaled to illegibility. **The real route is the client portal** — it's already a scrolling web page, so showing the estimate there and keeping the PDF for signing/attaching is the version of this that works. Scoped feature, not a tweak.

**`scripts/preview-presentation-estimate.tsx` is KEPT, not throwaway** — the only way to see this template without a live project. It renders three fixtures — **Kennedy 20 subs → 14 pages · Williams 12 → 5 · Dover 1 → 3**. ⛔ **The Kennedy fixture is VERBATIM from the real record and must stay that way.** My invented one had six short details per subproject where the real one has twelve including a 300-character paragraph; every bug in the list below was invisible until the fixture matched reality. **Re-run it and LOOK after touching the component**; react-pdf compiles happily while overlapping text.

**Mechanics:** resolution is most-specific-first — caller request → the project's **stamp** → org default → 'standard'. The stamp is what makes a regenerate reproduce the document the client already has. ⛔ **Every 095 column is read in an ISOLATED select** (route, modal, settings): PostgREST fails an entire select on one unknown column, so folding them into a shared read would blank a card or kill estimate generation pre-migration. **The settings autosave is gated on the 095 read having SUCCEEDED** — otherwise a pre-095 database would write 'standard' + `[]` over real settings the moment the migration landed.

**Why:** "We're sending out $600k estimates, it needs to look way better." Direction = the Timeline (timeline.com) editorial aesthetic. The approved mockup went through two markup rounds — the deltas from the first draft are LOAD-BEARING, don't resurrect them: stats OFF the cover (they're a small dotted-rule row under the thank-you now) · NO materials palette (auto-aggregation needs curation; each sub keeps its authored material line) · the cover's centerpiece is the **PACKAGE INDEX** (every subproject with its price, dot-leadered, summing to the total — works at 1 sub and at 20) · smaller type, generous white space. Claude-design references remain at `prototypes/design/estimate-presentation/`. This is a SECOND template — the standard estimate stays exactly as-is (Bam's and B2B's).

**Fonts — LOCKED (free set now, purchasable swaps later):** **Newsreader** 300 (headline serif + italic thank-you) · **Instrument Sans** 400/500/600 (labels, body) · **Space Mono** 400 (**every numeral**: index amounts, totals, schedule, mini-stats — the GT America Mono role; Andrew approved the mono-digit look specifically). Site truth for later purchases: numerals = **GT America Mono** (Grilli Type), body = **Suisse Intl** (Swiss Typefaces); the headline "Tool Serif" is CUSTOM to Timeline (FFF-Tool-SerifVF) and not for sale — Newsreader keeps that role unless Andrew picks a retail serif (Editorial New / Reckless Neue / Tiempos were offered). Layout must survive a straight font-file swap. react-pdf: vendor STATIC TTFs (no variable fonts) under `assets/fonts/`, `Font.register` in the PDF route; the `pdfText` glyph-sanitizer rule (`e771ddc`) applies.

**Document structure (per the approved mockup — ⚠️ SUPERSEDED IN PART by round-3 C redlines 2026-09-04: the cover's kicker meta line and the Prepared-for grid are DELETED, date lives in the run header, "Delivered to {address}" sits under the headline, and the scope pages open with a "The specifications" kicker. See "round 3" above; don't rebuild the cover from this list):**
1. **Cover** — run header (BUILT · EST-no / project) · kicker meta (EST no · date · valid 30 days) · editorial headline ("A custom millwork package for the Williams residence." — auto-generated from client/project, EDITABLE at send time) · one quiet total line · **the package index** (subproject name … dot leader … Space Mono amount; sum row; long tail groupable like the mockup's vanities row is a DISPLAY choice — v1 renders one row per sub, no grouping logic) · Prepared-for grid · run footer.
2. **Scope pages** — per subproject: Newsreader name + Space Mono amount on one baseline, plan-ref line, authored material/dimension line, details as two-column dotted rows, **"Installation included" under the price on the right**, Excludes on a footer line. No aggregated materials anywhere.
3. **The numbers** — total · payment schedule rows (mono % + amounts) · terms · "From the shop" closing note (Newsreader italic, signed) · **mini-stats row** (small, dotted-rule top: 2013 · 15 · 8 · 12 & 16 — org-configurable via `orgs.estimate_cover_stats` jsonb, empty = row omitted; despite the column name they live on the CLOSING page now) · acceptance lines.

**Template mechanics — per-estimate toggle (B2B keeps the standard):** migration **`095`**: `orgs.estimate_template_default` ('standard'|'presentation', default 'standard') · `projects.estimate_template` (nullable — stamped by the send-time choice so regenerate keeps it) · `orgs.estimate_cover_stats` jsonb. SendEstimateModal gets the picker (default = project stamp, else org default) + the editable headline sentence (persist alongside the template stamp so regenerate keeps it — e.g. `projects.estimate_headline`, same migration). Settings → Estimates card grows the default picker + stat rows. ⛔ `orgs` writes through `updateOrgChecked`; isolated selects per the wave-1 item-9 pattern (a pre-095 database must not blank the card).

**Build shape:** new `EstimatePresentationPdf.tsx` beside `EstimatePdf.tsx`, same data payload + the derived package index; the PDF route branches on template. No changes to the standard template's output. Verify: render Williams (EST-0010, 15 subs) side-by-side with the mockup, AND a 1-sub estimate (Dover vanity) to prove the cover index works small; print both.

### Small fixes wave 3 — scoped 2026-09-02 (Cowork pass with Andrew). **BUILD IN ORDER. Migration `094` (items 5+6) must run on prod before deploying those items.**

**BUILD STATUS 2026-09-03: wave 3 COMPLETE — all six items.**

**Item 1 ✅ IMPORTED LIVE 2026-09-03.** Hunt (re-import) · Bonzer $168,090/899h · Schiller Anderson & Kiran **Millwork Wal Opt** $49,075/379h · Schiller Brabson Dining Table $16,099/126h. Every one verified price + hours against Built; `refreeze-bid-totals.mjs` reports **12 × ok**.
- **⛔ `migration_id_map` OUTLIVES THE ROWS IT POINTS AT — guarded now (`5cf4e8a`), and it nearly ate two jobs.** Deleting a project to force a clean re-import leaves the map holding built_id → a dead uuid, so every upsert takes its `if (existing)` branch and updates by that id, matching **zero rows** — which PostgREST reports as SUCCESS. The run prints "updated 1" having written nothing and the job stays deleted. **Fourth appearance of this trap** (after `updateProjectName`, the `users` table, and the team merge). `lookupLiveMillsuiteId` now proves the row exists, clears the dead mapping and re-inserts. It fired on Hunt (project + 7 subs) **and on Bonzer (project + 3 subs), which nobody knew had been imported-then-deleted.**
- **⛔ UNEXPLAINED PRICE DRIFT ON A LIVE JOB — ANSWERED, AND THE ANSWER IS THE WORRYING ONE.** Hunt held **$107,152** in MillSuite against Built's **$115,122**. **Andrew confirmed 2026-09-03 that Built is correct and he did NOT revise it** — so the app silently wrote **$7,970 off a 90% job** at some point after it passed `12 × ok` on 2026-08-04. Re-import restored the right number, which also destroyed the evidence, so the mechanism is unknown. STATE already records one instance of this shape (a stale browser tab recomputing an imported project and writing its total back into `bid_total`).
  - **`refreeze-bid-totals.mjs` IS THE DETECTOR for this** — it compares every imported job's `bid_total` against its frozen line sum. **Run it after any session that touches imported jobs.** It's read-only without `--apply`. Nothing else catches a silent write-down; the number just looks like the price.
  - **Not yet diagnosed — a real open risk, not a closed incident.** It can recur on any imported project, and an under-priced job reads as normal.
- **⚠️ TWO manifest jobs are marked `import` but do NOT exist in MillSuite: `Jabal - Change Orders` ($4,890) and `Vallowe - Colquhoun Project` ($33,430).** Either never imported or imported-and-deleted. **Andrew's call** — they'd come in with one `--project` run each.
- **Remaining per-job follow-through (Andrew, manual):** record payments-to-date on the new jobs (milestones written: Hunt 3 · Bonzer 4 · Schiller 3 · Brabson 3 — these are PROJECTED receivables, not payments), link QB invoices, force production where Built already had approvals, place on /capacity + /schedule. **Hunt came back with a NEW project id**, so any QB link it had needs re-establishing.

**Andrew's live pass, once deployed:** tracked time reads "Xh Ym" on /me, /time and the project actuals (estimates stay decimal — that's correct, not a miss) · the Start button in /me → Other work is fully on screen on his phone · a job in production shows the fill bar, and one that's over shows red · **sell something and confirm "Sold today" appears on its card and "Marked sold" in the history drawer** (this is the only way to prove the new column is being written, since nothing was backfilled) · the clock icon in the project header opens the drawer and old jobs show their derived rows rather than nothing.

**Item 2 ✅ (`f2bcc58`) — tracked time reads "2h 34m".** There were **FOUR** formatters for the same thing (`fmtActualHours` 2.6h · `hoursLabel` 2h 34m · `formatHours` 2.6 hrs · `hrsFromMin` 2.6h), so the same minutes rendered three ways depending where you stood. Now one, in `lib/actual-hours.ts`. **⛔ ESTIMATED hours stay decimal and must NOT be routed through it** — an estimate of 2.5h is a judgement, "2h 30m" claims a precision nobody has. `/suggestions` shows both together (est 4.5h → act 5h 12m), which is where the distinction is most legible.

**Item 3 ✅ (`52aa234`) — the off-screen button, and why it happened.** It was the Start/Switch button in **Other work**: the project `<select>` is a flex item, and a flex item won't shrink below its widest OPTION by default, so a long project name pushed the button off the edge. `min-w-0` fixes it; the same bug was in the PTO date row. **⛔ Don't remove those `min-w-0`s.**
- **The four tab bodies moved to `components/me/tabs.tsx`. NEXT.JS FORBIDS EXTRA NAMED EXPORTS FROM A PAGE FILE** (a type error), so nothing in a `page.tsx` can be rendered against fixtures — which is why this was never caught. Same reason `StageStrip` moved to `components/project/`.
- Also fixed while looking: history rows showed **only a date**, so two entries on one day were indistinguishable and Edit/Delete acted on a row you couldn't identify. Tap targets raised to 44px; `ChangePassword` does it **only below `sm:`** because /settings shares it. ⚠️ **A container-width harness CANNOT test a `sm:` media query** — those follow the real viewport.

**Item 4 ✅ (`1ae267b`, redesigned `20411f9` after Andrew's live look) — the CONNECTOR leaving Production is the progress bar.** It fills green with tracked-vs-estimated, red past 100%. **The first cut drew its own 5px bar under the label AND left the connector, so it read as two stacked bars — and widened the node to `flex-[2.5]`, knocking the pips out of line with every other project.** Now: no extra element, same 2px height / `flex-1` / gap as every other connector, so a job in production has the same rhythm as one in any other stage. **The % is a tooltip, not a label** (Andrew's call — the strip answers "where is this job", and a permanent number competes with that); hovering gives percent + tracked vs estimated.
- Two guards worth keeping: **no estimate ⇒ no fill at all** (a full bar would read as "finished" rather than "nobody estimated this"), and an overrun **caps the fill at 100% width** — red carries the meaning, not length, so it can't paint past the next pip.

**Items 5+6 ✅ (`c95acdf`) — `sold_at` + the hidden timeline. Migration `094` ✅ ON PROD.**
- `sold_at` stamps on the **first** transition into sold only — re-dragging a card must not reset "Sold 12d ago". **No backfill on purpose**; pre-094 projects show nothing rather than a guessed date.
- **⛔ `recordProjectEvent` swallows every error by contract.** A missing timeline line is cosmetic; a stage change that throws because logging broke is an outage. **Never make a caller depend on its result.**
- **Wired:** stage changes, sold, production started (both paths — they share `commitProductionStart`), estimate marked sent, milestone payment received, rename. **NOT wired yet:** CO created/approved/signed, invoice pushed to QB. ⚠️ The milestone call sits **above** the invoicing branch because that branch **returns early in QuickBooks mode** — below it, every QB org (including Built) would log nothing.
- **The drawer merges recorded events with derived ones** from created_at/imported_at/estimate_sent_at/sold_at, so old projects don't open blank. **A real event suppresses its derived twin** — `sold_at` and the 'sold' event are written by the same path, so every future sale would otherwise show "Marked sold" twice.
- **Both pre-094 traps are handled:** `updateProjectStage` retries without `sold_at` on 42703, and `/projects` asks for it separately — inline it would have left the page **empty**, not merely unlabelled.

**6. Project timeline (hidden until asked for).** Same migration `094`: **`project_events`** (`org_id`, `project_id` FK cascade, `event_type` text, `label` text, `meta` jsonb, `actor_user_id`, `created_at`, org RLS). Events recorded AT THE WRITERS (one helper in `lib/project-events.ts`, best-effort — an event insert must never fail the main write): stage changes (incl. `startProduction` + force-start), estimate marked sent, deposit received (both paths: manual mark + QB watcher), payment recorded, CO created/approved/signed, invoice pushed to QB, project renamed. UI: **hidden by default** — a small clock/history icon in the project header opens a right drawer, reverse-chron, timestamp + label + who. The drawer also renders derived pseudo-events from existing columns (created, `imported_at`, `estimate_sent_at`, `sold_at`) so old projects aren't empty — derived rows styled slightly muted, no table writes for them.
   - Verify: sell a test project → sold_at stamps, card shows "Sold 0d ago", timeline shows the stage event; mark deposit → event appears; drawer stays invisible until the icon is pressed.

### Task system v1 — ✅ BUILT 2026-09-02 (`33dc2db`); migration `093` ✅ ON PROD. **Nothing blocking — only Andrew's live pass (list at the end).**

**`093_tasks.sql` ✅ RUN ON PROD 2026-09-02 by Andrew.** `tasks` + `task_comments`, org RLS on the `projects` FOR-ALL pattern (083), idempotent, ends with `NOTIFY pgrst`. Verified through PostgREST with the public key — both tables answer and every column is in the schema cache.
   - _Worth knowing: the first run silently produced nothing._ The file is wrapped in `BEGIN…COMMIT`, so a failure anywhere rolls back everything and you're left with no tables and no obvious sign — the check caught it because it asks for the tables by name rather than trusting "it ran". **Verify a migration landed before building on it**; the usual cause is the Supabase SQL editor running only the selected text.

**The shape follows the SHEET, not a generic todo app. Two things that look like omissions are the design — don't "fix" them:**
- **Buckets are a curated list, not a calendar.** Nothing auto-rolls. A stale Today item stays in Today until a human drags it, because Kaylin's daily pass IS the process — ageing rows for her would quietly remove the judgement that makes the list worth reading. There is deliberately no `due_date` and no scheduled job.
- **~~The default filter is All, not Mine.~~ REVERSED 2026-09-03 by Andrew (`9fc1a27`) — it lands on MINE.** The original reasoning (it's a master list, you should see what everyone carries) lost to the simpler one: what you personally owe is the question you open a task pane to answer. All is one click away and the choice still persists in localStorage.

**Decisions worth keeping:**
- **`assignee_ids` is jsonb, not a join table.** v1 never asks the database for one person's tasks — it loads the org's few dozen rows once and filters client-side, which is also why toggling Mine/All costs nothing. Promote it to a join table if that stops being true.
- **`project_id` nullable, `ON DELETE SET NULL`** — the sheet's standalone TASK rows are first-class, and a task outlives a deleted practice project.
- **Role-gated in the PROVIDER, not just the button.** A worker's session never fetches the org's task list at all; every surface renders nothing rather than an empty list that looks broken.
- **Every write selects the row back and throws on zero rows.** `tasks` does allow browser writes under its own policy (unlike `orgs`/`users`), but a blocked write would otherwise read as success — same guard as `updateProjectName`.
- **`TasksContext` is exported** so a dev harness can render the panel against fixtures. The panel needs auth + 093 to be reachable in the app, and a copied-markup mock would drift from what ships. Nothing in the app should consume it directly — use the hooks.

**⚠️ FIXED SAME DAY (`7da3804`) — the first cut couldn't assign anyone.** Andrew opened the drawer and asked how he was supposed to give Hunter a task. Two defects:
- **The New-task form had no assignee picker.** Assignment existed only inside an expanded row, so from the place you create a task there was no way to hand it to someone. There's a "Who's doing it" row in the drawer's create form now.
- **⛔ Worse: `listAssignees` read `users` and dropped `role='member'`,** so it only offered people with manager logins. **The sheet assigns work to PEOPLE, and most of a shop floor has no login.** Assignees now come from the **roster (`orgs.team_members`)**, so **`assignee_ids` holds ROSTER ids, not user ids** — "Mine" resolves the signed-in login to its roster entry, and shows nothing rather than everything if the owner isn't on the roster. Free to change outright because no tasks existed yet; it would be a data migration now.
- Reads `orgs.team_members` directly rather than via `loadShopRateSetup` on purpose — that helper also pulls salaries from the owner-only compensation table, which a task picker has no business touching.

**`/tasks` page — BUILT (`7da3804`), Andrew's call: page AND drawer.** Four buckets side by side with per-column add; linked from the drawer's "Full list →" and from Manage in the nav. **`TaskRow` was extracted to its own module** so the page and drawer render a task identically — two copies drift the moment either changes.

**"Gets tasks" per person — BUILT (`e64398a`), Andrew's ask ("managers and Hunter are really the only people that need tasks").** A checkbox beside Billable on each `/team` member; only people with it on are offered in the three pickers and the filter chips. No migration — `team_members` is jsonb, so it rides the existing debounced roster save.
- **Defaults TRUE on rows that predate the flag.** An empty picker reads as "assignment is broken" — exactly the bug this followed — so it ships with everyone on and Andrew trims it in one pass. **Andrew still needs to do that pass**; until he does, the picker lists the whole roster.
- **⚠️ `listAssignees` still RETURNS people who are switched off**, carrying `tasksEnabled` as a field; the UI filters on it (`pickable`). Dropping them from the list entirely would make anyone assigned *before* being switched off render as "Unknown" on their existing tasks and break Mine for them. **Pickers filter on the flag; name lookup uses the whole roster.**

**Three fixes 2026-09-03 (`9fc1a27`) from Andrew's live pass:**
- **⛔ THE BIG ONE — "Gets tasks" didn't save, and the way it failed is the lesson.** `lib/team-merge.ts` holds an **allowlist of fields a page may own**; `tasks_enabled` was added to `TeamMember` without being added to it. So the checkbox toggled, the autosave ran, the indicator said saved, the write succeeded — **and the merge copied the SERVER's value back over the field on the way out.** An unlisted field isn't rejected loudly, it's silently un-edited. **The allowlist is now `Record<Exclude<keyof TeamMember, 'id'>, true>`, so adding a field to `TeamMember` fails the build here until someone decides whether a page may own it.** `scripts/verify-team-merge.mjs` also grew a sweep asserting every field survives a merge (run it with `npx tsx` — it imports a `.ts` file, so plain `node` throws ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND). Both nets confirmed to catch the bug when the entry is removed. **Any future field on the roster: add it in BOTH places.**
- **The nav badge is personal now.** It counted every open task in the org, so it was permanently lit and signalled nothing.
- **⚠️ ONE FALLBACK RULE COVERS BOTH, and it's deliberate: a login with no linked roster row falls back to showing EVERYTHING.** Such a user can't be assigned anything, so a personal badge/filter would be a permanent, undiagnosable blank. Showing the whole shop makes the misconfiguration *visible* — **a badge counting everything means "this login isn't linked to a roster row on /team"**, which is actionable. It also covers the instant before the roster loads, so the panel never opens blank. **If Andrew reports the badge still counts everything, check the link before touching code.**
- Header subtitle now follows the filter (it was hardcoded "Everyone's list" — true on All, a lie on Mine). `myAssigneeId` moved into `TasksProvider` so the badge, panel and `/tasks` share one resolution instead of three copies.

**Project pickable AT CREATE TIME — BUILT 2026-09-04 (`1e3c87f`).** Andrew: "right now it makes you add a task, save it and then reopen and assign it to a project." A Project select now sits in **both** create forms (drawer + `/tasks`), between the buckets and the assignee chips. In the drawer it seeds from the panel's project filter and follows it, so a task started from a project's "Tasks · N" lands there without re-picking.
- **This is the SAME gap assignment had in the first cut** — the control existed only inside an expanded row, so the create form couldn't do the thing you open it to do. **Check any future task field against that pattern before shipping it.**

**⏸ STILL UNRESOLVED — "request" vs "assign".** Andrew asked to "assign or request other people to do tasks"; the request half (send it, they accept or decline) was never settled because the conversation turned to the assignment bug. Assigning now works. **Ask him whether he still wants a request/accept flow before building one** — it needs per-person state on the task, so it's a schema change, not a UI tweak.

**Surfaces:** `components/tasks/` — `TasksProvider` (one store; the nav badge, panel and project button all read it, so a task created on a project page updates the badge without a reload) · `TasksPanel` (buckets + collapsed Done, drag between buckets, row expands to edit title/bucket/project/assignees + the comments thread) · `TasksNavButton` · `ProjectTaskButton` ("+ Task" popover + "Tasks · N" opening the panel pre-filtered).

**Verified by rendering the real panel against fixture context:** all four buckets, project chips, a no-project "Task" chip, a three-assignee row, collapsed Done, and the expanded detail. `check-tour-targets` passes at 44 — the nav trigger deliberately carries **no** `data-tour` hook, since tour scripts point at nav items by stable identifier and a new one risks a collision.

**Left for Andrew (deploy first), with the sheet open beside it:** rebuild ~10 real rows across all four buckets including a no-project row and a 3-assignee row · drag between buckets (that's the daily pass) · leave a comment like "Ordered ETA 9/3" and confirm another manager sees it · "+ Task" from a project page lands pre-linked · Mine shows only yours · completing a task drops it into Done and unchecking restores it.

_Not in v1 (say no until asked again): notifications/mentions, recurring tasks, importing the sheet programmatically._ **⚠️ Hunter can be ASSIGNED but cannot SEE his tasks** — workers only get `/me`, which has no task surface. Deliberate for v1; it's the obvious next ask once the list is in daily use.

### Small fixes wave 2 — ✅ ALL ELEVEN BUILT (2026-09-01 → 09-02). **No migrations in the whole wave. Nothing left to build; Andrew's live pass on 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10 is at the end.**

**8. ✅ PRICING BUG FIXED — Solid Wood Top was under-billed twice over. `e3efbc4`.** No schema change. Two independent defects on the same product.
   - **Root A — BdFt came off the TYPED thickness.** A 1.5" finished top milled from 8/4 consumes **2" of rough stock** — that's what gets bought, jointed and planed. Andrew's 40×24 read 10 BdFt against a real 13.33. Because `scale` rides the same figure it under-counted **labor** too. BdFt now measures on the component's `quartersToInches(thickness_quarters)`; the typed field is **"Finished thickness"** — spec only, prices nothing. Falls back to the typed value only until a material is picked (the save gate requires one).
   - **⛔ The calibration must share that basis.** `calib_thickness_in` is now explicitly the cal piece's ROUGH thickness and the walkthrough says so ("2″ if you'd mill it out of 8/4"). Lines and the calibration on different bases skews the labor scale **silently** — this is the thing to check first if hours ever look wrong on this product.
   - **Root B — waste charged twice.** `materialPerPiece` already multiplies by the component's `waste_pct`, then the generic per-line waste was applied AGAIN on the inflated subtotal (his panel: "× 1.15 waste" then "Waste 5% · $15"). Solid-wood waste is a property of the **stock** — walnut yields differently from maple — so the component's % is the single source. The branch forces per-line waste to 0 and **the panel drops the Waste knob to match**; don't re-add that row without removing the zeroing. Consumables stays.
   - **`ComposerSolidWoodComponent` gained `thickness_quarters`** — the composer's view of a component had dropped it, which is exactly why the rough thickness wasn't reachable in the pricer.
   - **Verified with the real pricer** against his exact case: 2 pieces 40×24×1.5" finished in 8/4 Walnut @ $12.97 +15% → **26.67 BdFt total, $397.75 material** (his ≈$398), waste applied **once**, **6.0** labor hours.

**7. ✅ Solid wood into the Materials tab — `1d455fd`.** No schema change — **the model was always complete and always priced correctly** (migration 039, `lib/solid-wood.ts`). The whole bug was findability: the only UI was a collapsed synthetic group at the bottom of the Line items sidebar with a read-only detail pane, so Andrew reasonably concluded the feature didn't exist.
   - **New `components/rate-book/SolidWoodSection.tsx`**, mounted at the bottom of the Materials tab: grouped by species, one row per thickness, inline edit + add row (species datalist, 4/4 · 5/4 · 6/4 · 8/4 quick picks + custom quarters). **Its own section, not extra catalog rows** — it's priced per BOARD FOOT with a waste factor and shares no columns with a $/sheet material.
   - **`name` is derived** as `"4/4 Walnut"` rather than being a third field to keep in sync; species + thickness are the identity, `name` is just what the composer dropdown renders. Validation mirrors the walkthrough's (no negative cost, waste 0–99) — either would quietly poison every line priced off that stock.
   - **"Recalculate all" survived the move** and now sweeps every row rather than one. Editing $/BdFt does NOT reach linked door materials on its own; dropping that button would have stranded them.
   - **Composer dead end fixed:** the Solid Wood Top material dropdown said "add them in the rate book first" and left you to find your way back. It now opens `SolidWoodWalkthrough` in place and refreshes the dropdown — via a **callback to the parent**, which owns the mount, matching `onOpenFinishWalkthrough` rather than threading `orgId` down.
   - **Retired:** the sidebar group, `SolidWoodDetail`, and the rate-book page's walkthrough mount (which had no opener left once the group went). The walkthrough COMPONENT stays — it's the guided-create path, and the composer is now its only live entry point.

**1. ✅ Schedule backward paging — `c9a645b`.** ◀ / ▶ (4 weeks a step) + a Today jump, with a "3w BACK" chip while off-centre. Week headers read `THIS WEEK` / `2 WKS AGO` instead of labelling a past column "WK 1"; forward numbering is unchanged.
   - **The whole risk was the coordinate split — see the ⚠️ in CURRENT FOCUS.** Blocks stay ABSOLUTE and the window moves, so undo history and an in-flight what-if stay valid across a page. Three things had to stay pinned to the fixed origin, each a silent data-corrupter otherwise: `weekIndexToDate`/`dateToWeekIndex` (persistence), the drag's `startWeek` (converts view→absolute on pointer-down; the clamp is the visible window), and the AI prompt's week labels (it's handed absolute blocks and its answers are applied straight back).
   - Both views needed no restructuring — all positioning already funnelled through one `dept::week` keyed map.

**2. ✅ Mark schedule blocks complete — `f3488b6`.** `department_allocations.completed` had existed all along with nothing reading or writing it. A ✓ toggle on each block flips it: hover-revealed like the ⋮, pinned visible and green once set, block dimmed and label struck through. Works in Dept Flow and Swimlane.
   - **Deliberately inert in the math** — capacity, utilization, over-capacity shading and the AI context all still count a completed block's hours. Andrew asked for a visual aid; quietly dropping finished work out of capacity would change what the schedule means.
   - Optimistic write, rolls back on failure. **No `updated_at` in the payload — `department_allocations` doesn't have that column** (verified against the live schema; same trap as `subprojects`).

**3. ✅ Capacity double-count — `66d69fd`.** `deptDailyHoursByTeam` now splits a member's daily hours across their `dept_assignments` instead of adding the full day to each, so one person in assembly + install no longer advertises 16h from an 8h human. Shop-wide capacity is exactly headcount × hours again; `/schedule` and `/capacity` both inherit it through the one function.
   - **The shop rate is untouched on purpose** — `sumBillableHoursYear` counts each member once and was already right.
   - **Even shares are an approximation and the code says so.** Andrew's real pattern is time-varying ("a month on install, two days in the shop"), so this is right in aggregate and wrong for any given week. The truthful fix is the `[unscoped]` week-by-week crew assignment under Next — don't mistake this for it.

**4. ✅ Whole-row approval click — folded into 5 (`bb2f3e2`), as scoped.** The approval row is now a real button (click or Enter/Space) rather than a 16px chevron; the chevron stays as the cue. The expanded body `stopPropagation`s so an action click can't collapse the card under itself.

**5. ✅ Pre-production redesign — `bb2f3e2`, built to the approved mockup.** Sticky subbar + StagePill · white header (name + Pre-production chip, client/address pills, big mono total, deposit line) · gate strip leading with "N approvals to go" over material/drawing/deposit counts + progress bar · rounded subproject cards with hover borders, status badge and Approvals | Drawings columns · CO row + muted explainer.
   - **NEW sticky "What's left" punch list**, derived entirely from data already on the page (unapproved slots, unapproved drawings, deposit) so it can't disagree with the cards beside it. Clicking a row expands that slot and scrolls to it — that's why `ApprovalSlots` gained **`focusItemId`**, which is additive: it opens a card and never closes one the operator opened. Start production fires the same readiness-gated `startProduction()` the project page uses; `lib/project-stage` stays the only writer.
   - **`StagePill` + the cover-stage mapping moved to `components/project/StagePill`** and the project page imports it now. The pre-prod pill has to be the identical pill, and a second copy of "which stages collapse into bidding" is exactly the thing that drifts.
   - **Two guide steps were describing things the page no longer shows, and were corrected** (the spec asked for this check): the gate strip counts materials/drawings/deposit, not approved/in-review/pending; and "press the down arrow on a spec" is now "click a spec row to open it". `check-tour-targets` passes at 44 — `approvals-page`, `approval-gate`, `spec-list`, `drawings-approve`, `back-to-project` each still resolve to exactly one element.
   - **Layout defects found by Andrew's screenshot and FIXED — `fbd0c81`.** The first pass was compile-checked only, and four things were wrong on a real project: (a) **"Upload revision" was clipped** — `DrawingsTrack` puts its label AND both action buttons on one `justify-between` row, which can't fit the mockup's 260px column; the row now wraps, the column is **320px and only splits at `xl`**, and the buttons carry `whitespace-nowrap` so they break as whole buttons. (b) **Duplicate headings** — both child components print their own section header, which stacked under the card's new column heads; both gained an opt-in **`hideHeader`** (DrawingsTrack hides the LABEL only — its action buttons live on that same row). (c) **letter avatars removed** per Andrew: they read fine in the mockup and didn't on real names. (d) the gate strip's Deposit box ran off the right edge — the stat group wraps now. Also the headline said "1 approval to go" when the outstanding item was the **deposit**; it reads "1 item to go".
   - **⚠️ LESSON — compile-checking a layout proves nothing.** `tsc` + a 200 response said this page was fine while four things were visibly broken. Where a screen can't be reached locally (needs auth + real data), render its SHELL against fixtures through a throwaway route and actually look at it — that's how the fixes above were confirmed at 1440px, and it's the same reason the client portal split its views from data access.
   - Still Andrew's to confirm: the full page against the mockup with real specs and drawings on it.

**6. ✅ PRICING BUG FIXED — custom/vendor lines were contributing $0 to every total. `40d67c9`.** No schema change.
   - **Root, confirmed:** freeform lines store a per-unit `unit_price_override`; `computeLineBuildup` short-circuits those with every component bucket zeroed (correct — the price IS the price), so their money exists ONLY on `lineTotal`. `computeSubprojectRollup` built `subtotal` from the component buckets and never added it.
   - **Fix:** new **`customCost`** bucket, accumulated keyed off `unit_price_override != null` — **not** "lineTotal > 0", so a normal line can never be double-counted — included in `subtotal` and carried through `computeBucketedPrice` **grouped with material** (custom lines are bought goods, and it's where `optionsCost`, the other flat-dollars-no-hours bucket, already sits; grouping with labor would mark a vendor purchase up at the labor margin).
   - **⛔ `customCost` is REQUIRED on `CostBuckets`, not optional — leave it that way.** A money bucket that can be silently omitted is exactly how this bug survived. Making it required forced the compiler to surface all six construction sites (project page ×2, handoff, `project-totals`, `change-orders`, the Built migration script); an optional field would have let any of them keep quietly dropping it. Same reasoning applies to the next bucket anyone adds.
   - **Display:** "Custom & vendor" row on the subproject and project cost panels, hidden at zero.
   - **Verified with the real functions**, not by eye: two override lines add exactly **$360** to the subtotal and nothing when absent (so no double-count), and that $360 reaches `priceTotal` as **$553.85** — 360 / (1 − 0.35) — i.e. marked up like every other cost.

**9. ✅ Solid Wood Top calibrator moved to the rate book — `7bb1266`.** No schema change. Every other calibration (drawers, doors, features, finishes) already lived in the rate book; this one sat on Settings, away from the stock it prices against.
   - **New `TopCalibrationCard`** at the top of the Materials tab's Solid wood section. It says **more** than the Settings card did: the calibration piece + its BdFt, per-dept hours, edge multipliers, default cut method — or a plain empty state explaining what calibrating buys. Same `SolidWoodTopWalkthrough` behind the button.
   - **⚠️ `OP_DEPT` in that card must stay in step with `computeBreakdownSolidWoodTop`.** The summary re-derives per-dept hours (including the exclusive saw/CNC cut op) to display them; if the pricer's buckets ever move and this map doesn't, the card misreports real hours — worse than showing nothing. There's a comment on it saying so.
   - **Settings card + section removed outright.** Two user-facing strings still pointed there and were fixed: the save-gate refusal now names Rate book → Materials → Solid wood, and the composer's uncalibrated empty state — **another dead end that named a page and left you to navigate** — opens the calibrator in place via the existing pending-key mount, so finishing it resumes the pick you were making.
   - Verified all three card states rendered at 1280px; `check-tour-targets` passes and nothing targeted the Settings card.

**10. ✅ Kanban card signals — `757be89`.** No schema change; `estimate_sent_at` (060) already existed, stamped only by the project page's explicit "Mark as sent".
   - **⚠️ `SalesProject` now carries TWO stages and they are not interchangeable.** `stage` is the COLLAPSED board stage that picks a column (everything sold-and-beyond folds into one Sold column); **`project_stage` is the raw `projects.stage`** that decides what a sold card's badge says. **The drag handler updates both** — leaving `project_stage` stale made a card dragged to Sold announce itself as "Bidding" until the next reload.
   - **Sold cards show the real stage** (Pre-Production / In Production / Installed / Complete) instead of a "Live" badge that never changed. Label comes from the **shared** cover-stage mapping in `components/project/StagePill` — the pre-prod redesign extracted it precisely so a second copy couldn't drift, so reuse it, don't re-map.
   - **Estimate-sent chip** on pipeline cards, date in the tooltip. On a sold card the stage badge **replaces** it — a won job's estimate history isn't the headline.
   - **Auto-advance:** "Mark as sent" moves `new_lead` → `fifty_fifty` in the same handler. ⛔ **Guarded to `new_lead` only** — re-sending on a 90%/sold project must never demote it; the stage is the shop's judgement and this is the one place it could be silently overwritten. A failed stage bump logs rather than claiming the send failed, since the send already landed. Kanban drag stays free afterwards.
   - Verified the badge matrix rendered across all seven states (no chip on an untouched lead, chip on 50/50 and 90%, and each of the four sold stages).

**11. ✅ Kanban chip on its own row — `654a917`.** The chip shared a flex row with the price; five columns can't fit a big number and a long label on one line, so under roughly 1200px the chip spilled past the card edge ("ESTIMATE SEN…"). Both variants — stage label on sold, estimate-sent otherwise — now sit on their own left-aligned row under the price. Costs ~20px of card height, which is the cheaper trade. **The projects-page cards are a different layout and were deliberately left alone** (Andrew's call).
   - _Reproduced before fixing:_ a harness at the real board geometry (max-w-1400 page, px-6, `grid-cols-5 gap-3`, real card padding) is clean at 1440 but visibly overflows at **1120** and badly at **940** — the old layout only looked fine on a wide monitor, which is why it wasn't caught when item 10 shipped. The new one holds at all three.

### Client portal — ✅ BUILT, MIGRATED AND DEPLOYED 2026-08-31. **Nothing blocking; only Andrew's live pass (list at the end).**

Scoped in Cowork (brainstorm + prototype pass, then a final-design review with Andrew's v1 calls) and built the same day. Design source of truth: **`prototypes/design/client-portal-final.dc.html`** (Turn 3, "progress rail"; now committed, needs `support.js` + `assets/` beside it). Copy rules held to: no em dashes in client copy (use `·`), flat `#161614`, brass `#9A7B3F`, Archivo + Courier Prime.

**Migration + deploy both done 2026-08-31** — see the deploy verification in CURRENT FOCUS. Only the live pass is left.

**URLs:** `/portal/{token}` (client home) → `/portal/{token}/{projectId}`. Top-level, **not** `/{shop}/portal` — that's the worker login, and the two can't collide because `portal` was already in `RESERVED_SLUGS`. `/portal/` is registered public in `lib/auth-context.tsx`, so it never bounces to `/login`.

**1. ✅ Migration `092_client_portal.sql` — RUN ON PROD 2026-08-31 by Andrew.** Additive + idempotent + `NOTIFY pgrst`. Five pieces: `clients.portal_token` (+ `portal_token_issued_at`, partial-unique index) · `project_photos` (org-scoped RLS via `current_org_id()`) · the **public** `shop-photos` bucket · `projects.finishing_at` · `change_orders.signed_name/at/ip/pdf_url`.
   - **Token lives on `clients`, not a `portal_tokens` table** (the migration's "Code's call"). Access is per client, and revoke/regenerate is just overwriting the column. A table would buy multi-token/expiry/audit, none of which v1 wants. Promote it if per-project or expiring links ever land.
   - **No RLS policy grants `anon` anything here, deliberately.** A "read clients by token" policy would put the whole row one crafted PostgREST query away. The portal reads only through service-role server routes.
   - Bucket is **public read** to match `invoice-pdfs`/`org-logos` — the portal already links straight at public `invoice-pdfs` URLs for estimates and COs, and paths carry two uuids. Writes all go through the service role, so no storage policy is needed.

**2. ✅ Read model — `lib/client-portal.ts` (`af73483`).** Server-only, service role, one explicit field allowlist. See the boundary warning at the top of this file before touching it. Project reads are re-scoped to the token's client, so a valid token asking for another client's `projectId` 404s. Phases derive from `stage` + deposit (`amount_received > 0` or `deposit_override`) + drawings (`subproject_approval_status`) + `finishing_at`; **the current phase is the first one that isn't done**, so a job whose drawings are finished but whose deposit hasn't landed correctly reads as sitting on Deposit.
   - **Install target + "Started" come from `department_allocations.scheduled_date`, not a stored stage timestamp** — nothing stamps "production began", but `startProduction()` seeds allocations at that moment, so the earliest one *is* the start; install = the earliest allocation whose dept name matches `/install/i`. Both return null freely and drop their row rather than showing a guess.
   - **The design's 62% part-filled rail segment was not built.** Nothing in the schema knows how far through a phase a job is, and a made-up percentage on a client-facing page is the exact failure this portal exists to avoid. Current segment is solid brass. If a real signal ever lands (allocation hours done / total), `ProgressRail` in `components/portal/ui.tsx` is where it goes.

**3. ✅ Pages (`ed9bd56`) + view split (`bea7728`).** React **Server** Components; routes are thin (resolve → load → notFound) and all markup lives in `components/portal/PortalHomeView` + `PortalProjectView`. **That split is what makes the screens checkable** — the portal is unreachable locally without a live token against a migrated database, so both views were rendered against fixture data and read at 375px and 1280px. Keep them free of data access so that stays possible.
   - Own route group `app/(portal)` so it gets Archivo + Courier Prime and the portal palette without the app's nav or the marketing dark theme; the fonts load there and nowhere else.
   - **One deliberate layout deviation, noted in the file:** the design puts "FOR YOUR REVIEW" top-right on desktop and second-from-top on mobile. Two column stacks can't do both, so it sits in the left rail under the hero — mobile matches the design exactly, desktop still has it as the darkest thing above the fold. Changing that needs explicit grid placement, not an `order` utility.
   - Three things the render check caught and fixed: the browser tab read "MillSuite — Project Profit Tracker" on a client-facing page (now `generateMetadata` titles it with the shop's name); "New contract total" fought the +$2,130 figure for one line at 375px; and the payment schedule painted "Due on schedule" in brass, the attention colour, which now needs a real due date.

**4. ✅ The two writes (`af73483`).** Both re-prove the token server-side and return an identical 404 for unknown-token / not-your-project / no-such-project, so a stranger can't tell them apart.
   - **approve** re-implements `applyTransition()` on the service role — state flip **+ the `item_revisions` audit row + linked-slot propagation**. It can't call `lib/approvals.approve()` because that runs on the browser client. **If `applyTransition` grows a fourth step, mirror it here or portal approvals quietly diverge from in-app ones.** Idempotent, and refuses an item whose ball isn't with the client.
   - **sign-change-order** records the signature and renders a countersigned PDF (separate storage path, so the blank-signature copy already sent isn't overwritten), and **stops there** — see the consent-vs-money warning at the top of this file. `ChangeOrderPdf` gained an optional `signature` prop; dynamic strings go through `pdfText`. PDF failure is caught and logged: the signature is already recorded, and a render error must not tell a client their signature didn't take.

**5. ✅ The shop's side (`e5c7799`).** A "Client portal" panel on the project page under Documents: Copy portal link (+ Regenerate, confirm-gated because it dead-links every URL already sent), shop-photo upload with captions + thumbnails + remove, and the Finishing toggle. Change-order cards now show **"Signed by the client: name · date"** plus the countersigned PDF, and on a still-`sent_to_client` CO they say outright to approve it below to apply the change — that instruction is the whole reason the portal doesn't flip the state itself.
   - Photo uploads go through `/api/projects/[id]/photos` on the service role; the row inserts only after the object lands, and a failed insert removes the object, so a bucket orphan can't become a broken image on a client-facing page.
   - **`finishing_at` is DISPLAY ONLY** — grep-verified that nothing outside the portal and its toggle reads it. `stage` stays the single source of truth. The toggle only enables in production.

**Both open calls — CLOSED by Andrew 2026-08-31:**
- **"Finishing" phase** → **manual toggle** on the project page (`projects.finishing_at`). Not derived from the schedule: that can be wrong and can't be corrected.
- **QB payment link** → **no Pay button in v1.** There is zero payment-link code in the repo; QB's `InvoiceLink` is a separate API call returning links that expire, so it's real work with an uncertain payoff. Payments show what's been received and what's scheduled, plus a line saying the shop will invoice when each payment comes due. Revisit only if Andrew wants it.

**✅ Confirmed working live by Andrew 2026-08-31:** the portal opens on a real token, the client home lists his project, the project page renders, photos upload and appear in the feed. Everything below is what's still unchecked.

Shop photos should also open full size when tapped, and the Built wordmark in the header should no longer be cropped to "bui".

### Small fixes wave — ✅ ALL 10 ITEMS BUILT 2026-08-27 (1–3 + 6 confirmed live by Andrew; migrations `090` + `091` both on prod). **Nothing left to build and nothing blocking the deploy** — only Andrew's live pass on 4, 5, 7, 8, 9, 10, list at the bottom of this section.

**Migration `091_org_estimate_closing_note.sql` ✅ RUN ON PROD 2026-08-27 by Andrew.** Additive + idempotent: one nullable text column (`orgs.estimate_closing_note`) + a schema reload. Verified through PostgREST with the public key (schema-only, `limit=0`) — the column is visible, so the cache reloaded. Nothing depends on it except item 9's Settings card and the PDF's Thank-you section. The Settings read stays isolated in its own select so local/preview databases without it still load the page.

**Migration `090_material_fields.sql` ✅ RUN ON PROD 2026-08-27 by Andrew.** Additive + idempotent: two nullable text columns (`materials.category`, `materials.thickness`) + a schema reload. Nothing prices off them. Verified through PostgREST with the public key (schema-only, `limit=0`) — both columns visible, so the cache reloaded. The code also survives an un-migrated environment: a "column does not exist" on the first read flips `lib/materials.ts` to the pre-090 column list for the rest of the page's life, so it degrades to "no category/thickness" rather than a blank catalog. That net still covers local/preview databases.

**1. ✅ Project rename — `6d16258`.** `updateProjectName(projectId, name, orgId?)` in `lib/sales.ts` (trims, rejects empty, `.eq('org_id')` when passed) + a hover-revealed pencil on the project-page `<h1>` → input, Enter/check saves, Escape cancels, toast on both outcomes. **The write selects the id back and treats zero rows as a failure** — the same success-shaped-silence trap the `users` warning at the top describes; `projects` does have a FOR ALL RLS policy (083) so the browser write is legitimate, but the guard is cheap. Nothing to sync: kanban cards, dashboards, the QB modal and the PDFs all read `projects.name` at render time. Header only, no kanban-card rename (Andrew's call).

**2. ✅ Materials catalog organization — `d67230a`** (+ migration `090`, on prod; `089` stays reserved for the annual_comp blob cleanup). `lib/materials.ts` carries `category`/`thickness` through the type and every read/write, with the pre-090 fallback. `MaterialsCatalog` groups rows by category in ONE table (heading rows, uncategorized last, so columns stay aligned), adds single-select filter chips for thickness + use, shows thickness as a chip on the row, and takes both fields in the add form and inline editor from a datalist of the values the org already uses — free text, no vocabulary, no lookup table. **The datalists are hoisted to the component root**: `DraftFields` renders for the add form AND every open row editor, so inlining them would have duplicated the ids — same shape as the `data-tour` duplicate caught during the rate-book tour build. Composer dropdowns untouched; the browse-all grouping nice-to-have was left undone. _Materials created from the composer's "+ Add new" land uncategorized until Andrew tags them — expected, not a bug._

**3. ✅ Collapsible subproject scope — `d880979`.** `ScopeEditor` is now a chevron header (Change Orders pattern) carrying `activity type · N description blocks · N exclusions`, collapsed by default, counting only blocks with real text. Client state, no persistence, no schema change; the QB push path reads the same fields it always did and `ScopeEditor` carries no `data-tour` hooks, so nothing tour-side moved. Most of the diff is reindentation — `git diff -w` is 37 lines.

**4. ✅ Drawer edit-modal truth-up — `fb85606`.** No schema change. **`lib/rate-book-v2.ts` now owns the one answer to "which columns does the composer price this item from"** — `laborHoursColumn` / `laborHoursFor` / `hardwareCostFor` / `isDrawerItemType`, keyed on the category's `item_type`. Drawers → `drawer_labor_hours_*` + `drawer_hardware_cost`; everything else → the base columns. `RateBookItemRow` now types the five drawer columns it was already getting from `select('*')`. In the modal: labor fields bind per-dept through `laborHoursColumn`, **Install drops out for drawers** (no such column), Hardware $ is enabled and labelled "/ drawer", plus a note that hours are per drawer. Change history needed no wiring — `updateItem` diffs whatever it's handed.
   - **Went past the letter of the spec in two places, both the same bug:** (a) `computeBuildup` takes the item type, so the **Current tab** no longer shows a calibrated drawer as 0 hr / $0 hardware; (b) the **roster "ø uncalibrated" pill** reads through `laborHoursFor`, so drawers stop being flagged uncalibrated forever. Both were the same "reads base columns" mistake the modal had.
   - **⚠️ Non-obvious trap found while building — worth knowing before touching this modal again.** The draft holds BOTH column sets so the inputs bind cleanly, but `updateItem` blanket-applies whatever it's handed across the **Apply to** scope. Sending both sets meant an "all items in the shop" edit on a cabinet would write its zero drawer columns over **every drawer's calibration**. `save()` strips the inactive set before the call. Keep that strip if you refactor.

**5. ✅ Finishes-tab truth-up — `8359a18`.** No schema change. New **`lib/finish-breakdown.ts`** owns the shape + read/write of `rate_book_finish_breakdown`; **`components/rate-book/FinishBreakdown.tsx`** replaces the whole generic buildup for `item_type==='finish'`: base / upper / full each showing `hr/LF × shop rate` + material $/LF and the real per-foot cost, inline-editable, with the Doors-tab pointer for per-door finishes. **The wizard's `upsertBreakdown` is now a one-line alias over `saveFinishBreakdown`** so the two writers can't drift.
   - **Same past-the-spec fix, and this one matters:** `ensureFinishItem` never writes base labor hours, so a finish's base columns are *always* zero — **every finish showed the "ø uncalibrated" pill forever**, however well calibrated. Finish rows now answer from a set of ids with non-zero breakdown rows (`loadCalibratedFinishItemIds`, one extra query per page load).

**6. ✅ "Interior finishes" naming clarity — `a474c25`.** No schema change. The two-slot model is unchanged and correct: **interior** = box inside, per LF, per base/upper/full (`rate_book_finish_breakdown`) · **exterior** = doors/fronts, per door, on the door type (`door_type_material_finishes`). Only the naming moved.
   - **Tab is now "Interior finishes."** Detail pane leads with what it prices (inside of the box, per LF, rate varies by cabinet type); item 5's Doors-tab pointer now says outright that door finishing **isn't** priced there and that both apply to the same line — interior by the foot, doors by the door. **Also renamed the Doors tab's finish list to "Door finishes · per door"** (not in the spec, but it's the other half of the same confusion). Composer copy already said "interior finish" everywhere, so it agrees. No tour step names the tab; `check-tour-targets` still PASSes at 44.
   - **⚠️ The exterior branch was NOT merely dead — it was actively producing the confusion.** `FinishWalkthrough`'s `application` prop **defaulted to `'exterior'`**, and its "Duplicate as Exterior" button existed to create four blank exterior combo rows. Post-038 nothing can price those, so the button's only live effect was manufacturing dead rows in this very tab — a plausible source of Andrew's stray finish items. Button, handler and state deleted; `application` narrowed to `'interior'` at all three call sites (walkthrough prop, rate-book `finishWtApp`, composer `finishWtApp`/`openFinishWalkthrough`) so the branch is **unrepresentable, not just unreached**. Header comments + walkthrough copy say interior.
   - ~~Still open — legacy `application='exterior'` rows~~ **RESOLVED into item 7:** hide `application!=='interior'` rows from the tab (a tab named Interior finishes shouldn't list rows nothing can price). Andrew's live screenshot shows exactly his 4 items, so likely none exist anyway.
   - ~~Not code: Andrew renames his four legacy finish items~~ **SUPERSEDED by item 7** — renaming alone can't fix it while the walkthrough re-mints the door-style names.

**7. ✅ Flat interior-finish set — `a9abbfc`.** No schema change. `COMBOS` is now **Clear** (lacquer) · **Stain + clear** (stain, lacquer) · **Paint** (primer, paint) · **Gloss paint** (primer, paint) — no door-style dimension, since the box inside is flat either way. Base/upper/full calibration and the ÷8-from-an-8'-run mechanics are untouched. Walkthrough copy + header comment lost all door-style framing; the composer's "Pre-seeded finish combos" comment updated. **Nothing else seeds the legacy names** (grep for "on slab"/"on shaker" returns only the rename map + comments).
   - **The four canonical names + the rename map now live in `lib/finish-breakdown.ts`** (`INTERIOR_FINISH_NAME`, `LEGACY_FINISH_RENAMES`), not inline in the walkthrough — `ensureFinishItem` matches on them, so they're load-bearing strings.
   - **In-code rename, as preferred:** `renameLegacyFinishCombos(orgId)` folds the old four 1:1. **It only touches a row when BOTH hold: the row is uncalibrated (no non-zero breakdown row) AND the target name is free in that org** — so it can never relabel real numbers or collide with a row already migrated/hand-renamed. Idempotent; called from the rate-book load AND `ensureAndLoadFinishData`, so it self-heals every org (incl. pro-plus-test) with no migration and no manual step.
   - **Perf note if you touch `refreshAll`:** the rename is gated behind `hasLegacyFinishName` over the items already in memory, and only re-reads when it actually changed something. Don't hoist it back to an unconditional `await` before the load — that's a permanent round-trip on every rate-book open for a one-time fixup.
   - **✅ Closes item 6's open call:** `application!=='interior'` rows are now filtered out of the Interior finishes tab. Nothing has been able to price them since exterior finishing moved to the door type, so listing them only invited calibrating a dead rate. They're hidden, not deleted — the rows are still in the table if they're ever wanted.

**8. ✅ Subproject rename — `04cb629`** (Andrew asked for it directly, 2026-08-27: "like the project title"). No schema change. `updateSubprojectName(subprojectId, name, orgId?)` in `lib/sales.ts` beside `updateProjectName`, same guards (trim, reject empty, org-scoped, select the id back so a zero-row update can't read as success), and the identical pencil-on-the-`<h1>` affordance on the subproject page.
   - **⚠️ `subprojects` has NO `updated_at` column** — verified against the live schema (PostgREST 42703; `saveSubprojectDefaults` and `ScopeEditor.persist` both omit it too). `projects` DOES have one, so the two rename helpers legitimately differ. Setting it here fails the whole update at runtime with nothing for tsc to catch — don't "tidy" it back in.
   - **The subproject tab bar caches sibling names** (`siblingSubs`), so the save patches that array as well; without it the renamed subproject's own tab keeps the old name until a reload.
   - Audited before building: nothing snapshots the name. Schedule, worker time, CO PDFs and the approvals roster all read `subprojects.name` at render time (`approval_items` drops `subproject_name` before insert), and the QB push builds its line from activity type + scope description. So a rename propagates by itself, including on already-pushed jobs.

**9. ✅ Estimate closing note — `8aeccab`** (+ migration `091`, on prod). `orgs.estimate_closing_note`, nullable. Settings gets an **Estimates** card with its own autosave through `updateOrgChecked`. The PDF tail is now three consistently-headed sections — **Payment schedule → Terms → Thank you** — all using `smallLabel`; an empty note renders nothing, so an org without one gets today's PDF unchanged. Read org-live rather than from `estimate_snapshot_json` (standing shop copy, not per-estimate terms) and there's deliberately no request override.
   - **Two things kept separate on purpose, don't "tidy" either:** (a) the closing note has its **own select** in Settings — folded into the invoicing query, a pre-091 database fails the *entire* select (PostgREST rejects the whole thing on one unknown column) and blanks every invoicing field on screen; isolated, it just reads empty. (b) `estimateSave` is registered in the page's **aggregate save indicator** — an autosave left out of that list is a write whose failure, including the org-RLS silence `updateOrgChecked` exists to catch, is invisible to the user. There's now a comment on the aggregate saying so.
   - **Andrew pastes Built's copy into Settings after deploy** (no data migration). Approved text, verbatim:
     > Thank you for trusting us with your project. Built has been family owned since 2013. When you work with us, you're supporting 15 craftspeople, 8 families, 12 kids, and 16 pets — and every one of them takes pride in what leaves this shop. We'd be honored to build for you.

**10. ✅ Estimate line-description readability — `51e1fec`.** No schema change. Scope now renders as labelled sections — **MATERIAL / DESCRIPTION / DIMENSIONS / DETAILS / EXCLUSIONS** — with real spacing, exclusions as an em-dash list, "Includes installation" on its own line.
   - **⚠️ Built the OPPOSITE way to the spec, deliberately — read this before "fixing" it.** The spec said pass structured fields on the payload. That would have improved *freshly generated* estimates only: sent estimates re-render from `projects.estimate_snapshot_json`, which stores the **flattened `lineItems` and nothing else**, so every estimate already sent — EST-0010 included, the one in the verify step — would have kept rendering the old blob forever. Instead `parseRichDescription` reads the flat string back into sections, which fixes both paths, needs no payload/API/snapshot change, and keeps ONE source of truth so the QB line and the PDF can't drift. Cost: the parser is coupled to `buildRichDescription`'s format — **they live side by side in `lib/subproject-description.ts` and must be edited together.** Unrecognised text falls through as one unlabelled block, i.e. exactly what rendered before.
   - _(Spec correction for the record: the flatten does NOT live in the PDF API route — it's `buildRichDescription(sub)` in the project page's `qbLines` build. Also the push-modal comment claiming the spec is "editable in the preview" is stale; `QbPushModal` only displays it.)_
   - "Dimensions - …" / "Details - …" arrive as ordinary detail blocks and get promoted to their own sections off a **fixed keyword list** — not a generic `Word - value` match, which would invent headings out of real sentences.
   - **Both react-pdf traps re-checked, not assumed.** The cell is still a single `<Text>` with nested runs (spacing via newlines, never margins) — the nested `<View>` that made rows overlap is still a trap and now says so in the comment. Verified by rendering the real component through a throwaway route and rasterising the result: sections visibly separated, every text baseline strictly increasing (no overlap), a long multi-line item still paginates, and a line with no structured scope falls back to plain text.

### Rate book overhaul — scoped 2026-07-23 (Cowork deep-dive + design pass with Andrew; demo-feedback item 2). Build AFTER the fix list below is done.

**The pricing model (Andrew's mental model, confirmed — build to this):** a **calibrated item** (base cabinet, floating shelf) holds **labor hours per unit** + **material CONSUMPTION per unit** (how much it eats: sheets/LF, sheets/door). The **materials catalog** holds **prices** ($/sheet). Composer math = qty × consumption × chosen material's price. Today consumption is split messily (product constants in `lib/products.ts` + per-material `sheets_per_lf` on `rate_book_carcass_materials`) — the rebuild puts consumption on the calibrated item, price on the material.

**Key audit facts (Cowork exploration 2026-07-23, file refs):** composer material pricing reads the pools (`rate_book_carcass_materials`, `door_type_materials` (038), back-panel rows in `rate_book_items`) via `lib/composer.ts` ~563/582 — it NEVER reads `rate_book_items.sheets_per_unit`/`sheet_cost` for composer products (those Edit-modal fields are dead for them). Door labor lives on `door_types` (4-step `DoorStyleWalkthrough`); drawer labor on `rate_book_items.drawer_labor_hours_*` (5-step wizard); finishes on `rate_book_finish_breakdown`. Products + consumption constants are HARDCODED in `lib/products.ts` (`active:false` marks the Drawer/LED stubs). Dead/unwired: rate-book "Changes" tab (placeholder), `rate_book_options` (nothing reads), legacy inactive door_style rows (038 kept them), "Used in N jobs" counter (verify what feeds `confidence_job_count` or drop it).

#### Build in this order (each lettered chunk = own commit set; migrations idempotent + run on prod first):

**A. Cleanup (quick) — ✅ DONE 2026-07-23 (`d81794b`, tsc clean; no schema change; pending Andrew's live eyeball).** All six items:
- **Drawer + LED tiles removed from the "Add a line" picker** — dropped `'drawer'`/`'led'` from `PRODUCT_ORDER` (`lib/products.ts`). Their keys stay in `PRODUCTS`/`ProductKey` so legacy estimate lines + the parser paths (`sales.ts`, `pdf-parser.ts`, `parser-slot-resolver.ts` all reference `'drawer'`) still resolve a label. LED returns as a feature in chunk D.
- **Dead material fields hidden in Edit-item for composer-driven items** — new `COMPOSER_DRIVEN_ITEM_TYPES` set (`cabinet_style`/`door_style`/`drawer_style`/`finish`) → `isComposerDriven` hides the Material-mode selector + all sheets/$-sheet/linear/lump blocks and shows a short "priced per line in the composer" note. Labor hours stay editable. Freeform (`custom`) + `hardware` items keep their material fields.
- **Placeholder "Changes" tab dropped** — item detail is now Current | History only (tab type, buttons, body, header comment all updated).
- **Legacy door_style rows hidden from the roster** — filtered the whole `item_type==='door_style'` category out of `filteredTree`. Post-038 the live door styles are `door_types` (edited via the DoorStyle walkthrough from the composer); the old door_style `rate_book_items` are all inactive anyway. _(Went one step past "hide rows" → hid the dead category. Note: the `composer-loader` header comment claiming door styles load from door_style rate_book_items is STALE — it actually reads `listDoorTypes` from `door_types`.)_
- **"Used in N jobs" KEPT — feed verified.** `/suggestions` "Re-scan" → `regenerateSuggestions` → `bumpItemConfidence` writes `rate_book_items.confidence_job_count` from closed-job time-tracking evidence (same feed drives the ConfidencePill beside it). Real feed, so kept.

### Guided walkthroughs — RESTRUCTURE to PATH + SHELF. **Guides page ✅ REWORKED 2026-08-13 (`7549513`).** Spec: `specs/walkthroughs/v1-tours.md` ("RESTRUCTURED" section).

**Andrew's correction:** guides came out as a random menu; the teaching is LINEAR — you can't price a job before the rate book has materials and a door type. Rework the Guides page into two sections:
1. **THE PATH** — 6 ordered steps (shop setup → rate book → first job → sell it → team on the clock → getting paid), **gated by REAL APP STATE, not tour attendance** (the unlock/complete checks are specified per step in the spec file — e.g. first-job stays locked until ≥1 carcass material + ≥1 calibrated door type + base-cabinet labor exist). Locked steps visible with the reason + a link to the unlocking step. Journey UI: "2 of 6 complete," one obvious next action. Users who do setup without the tour auto-complete steps (facts, not attendance).
2. **THE SHELF** — unordered, always available: capacity · schedule · reports · COs · deep-dives · worker app.
- **New tour script "Set up your rate book" (path step 2, 8 steps) is drafted in the spec file** — build it; the first-job tour now chains FROM it and is state-locked without it. Sell-it/team/paid tour scripts come from later Cowork drafting passes; their path slots render locked with "guide coming soon" if the state gates aren't met, or auto-complete if they are.
- Existing v1 pieces (engine, both tours, checklist, practice projects, auto-offer) all carry over — this reshapes the Guides page + adds gating + one tour, it does not rebuild the engine.

**✅ DONE 2026-08-13 — the Guides page itself.** `/guides` is now Path (journey UI: progress bar, "N of 6 complete", one "Next up" flag on the first open unfinished step) over Shelf (8 placeholder cards). `PATH`/`SHELF`/`resolvePath()` live in `lib/walkthroughs.ts`; **`/api/guides/path` answers all six gates in ONE round trip, server-side** — two checks join across tables the browser can't reach in one hop (`estimate_lines` and `client_invoice_payments` both lack `org_id`, so they need `!inner` embeds). **All seven queries were verified to parse against the live schema before shipping** — a wrong embed would have been swallowed by the per-check error tolerance and reported "not done" forever. Each check is independently fault-tolerant, so a database missing a table (`materials` is 072+, invoices 041+) answers false for that one gate instead of blanking the page.
- **Judgement call, flag if wrong:** "a team login" = `users` with `role != 'owner'`. Counting all users would mark step 5 done for every org on day one, since the owner's row is created with the org.
- Path steps 4–6 and the entire shelf render **"Guide coming soon"** — they still track and **auto-complete from real work**, exactly as specced.
- **✅ "Set up your rate book" tour BUILT 2026-08-13 (`02755c6`)** — 8 steps verbatim from the spec + all 7 `data-tour` hooks. Path step 2 now has a real guide. **The rate book's sections are TABS (client state, no URL)**, so the tour points at the tab and lets the user press it rather than faking navigation — which works out better, because each tab switch is a clean signal (the next step's target only exists once that view is showing). Steps 2, 3 and 5 are therefore action steps with no buttons of their own. Step 3 → 4 rides the same mechanism: opening the add form is what reveals the "Shows in" checkboxes step 4 explains.
- **Duplicate caught before shipping:** the "Shows in" block lives in a shared `DraftFields` rendered for the add form AND every row being edited, and opening the add form does NOT close an open editor. Inline tagging would have put two identical `data-tour` values in the document. The hook is a **prop**, passed only by the add form.
- **`node scripts/check-tour-targets.mjs`** — run it after touching any `data-tour` or tour script. Fails on a target that resolves to zero tags OR more than one. Currently 19 targets, all resolve, no duplicates. (It earned its keep immediately by catching a real gap on first run.)

### Tour engine: snap-then-glide — ✅ 2026-08-14 (`fb907f4`, round 2 of the jump fix)

Andrew still saw jumps at sell-it 4→5 and 5→6: the PAGE keeps reacting after the action that advanced the step (reload removes the just-clicked deposit button and reflows the action row; the pre-prod page's banner shifts as data loads), and the card glided after those late reflows. Now every step has a **700ms grace window where card + ring track position changes instantly** (snap); gliding turns on only after the layout settles. Scroll-settle needs 3 stable frames. Engine-level — covers all guides.

### Tour engine: card no longer jumps between steps — ✅ 2026-08-14 (`2c340bb`)

Andrew: "the modal jumps around between selections." Two roots: (1) measuring mid-scroll caught targets off-screen → ring dropped → card flashed at the dock corner, then leapt to the target when the smooth scroll landed. `waitForScrollSettle()` now holds the step until the target's rect is stable and visible (800ms cap). (2) Step changes GLIDED the card/ring across the page. Both are keyed by step index now: step change = cut to the new position with a 200ms fade-in; within-step tracking still animates.

Andrew's call after the third live run: event-per-state steps made the cards fight the work. "Approve the materials" is now ONE look-step ringing the spec list with a numbered 5-item sequence (engine gained **`TourStep.bullets`**, rendered as an ordered list in the card) and a closing line: approve all the others, then press Next. Then: drawings (event) → "Everything is green lit" look at the gate banner → "Head back" DO on Back to project (advances when Start production appears) → start production → closer. `ms:spec-submitted`/`ms:spec-approved` stay wired in ApprovalSlots, currently unscripted.
